# Blog queue + lesson backlog — the single repository

Two sections. **§A** is the FIFO publication queue (the Wednesday bot takes the
FIRST `[queued]` block). **§B** is the comprehensive backlog: EVERY issue,
failure, and decision across ALL projects, past and future, with priority.
Topics get promoted §B → §A by writing a topic block. Every work session that
produces a lesson appends to §B (or straight to §A if it's clearly a post).
Format rules: PIPELINE.md.

---

# §A — Publication queue (FIFO)

## [published 2026-07-08] my-code-auditor-scored-1-00-on-fixtures-and-0-00-in-the-wild
**Angle:** The honest-engineering arc of the code auditor's real-world validation: seeded fixtures measured precision 1.00, then 13 real OSS Python web apps produced 276 findings with ZERO true positives (0.00). The five rule bugs real code exposed (SSRF self-taint on `requests.get().json()`, `.loads` over-match, positional sink arg-focus vs bound params, no reachability model, silent SAST drop), the fix pass that cut noise −90% (20→2) and still honestly found nothing, the seeded-CVE oracle 2×2 (TPs preserved 20/20 votes, obvious FP rejected 20/20, subtle value-provenance FP a 40% coin-flip CoT doesn't fix), and the retracted CoT mirage (harness token-cap + fallback parser — measure your measurer). Repos anonymized repo-A…M; no planted-vuln locations. **FULL POST ALREADY WRITTEN at src/content/blog/my-code-auditor-scored-1-00-on-fixtures-and-0-00-in-the-wild.md — publish as-is (flip this block, verify build); adapt promo drafts from it, do not rewrite the post.**
**Sources:** SovereignSec private repo bench/results/REALWORLD_VALIDATION_v1.md + REALWORLD_VALIDATION_v2.md + ORACLE_REALWORLD_v1.md; backlog OPS-17.
**Targets:** linkedin, reddit:r/netsec, hn

## [queued] the-model-echoed-my-prompt-back
**Angle:** A real manga page lost ALL its dialogue because Nemotron returned `"dialogues[]"` — the prompt's array notation — as literal JSON keys, and `.get("dialogues")` silently got nothing. Model output variance as a distribution: same prompt at temp 0.1 produced pixel bboxes, normalized floats, plain keys, bracket keys, and occasionally no JSON at all (json_repair returns a bare string; callers .get() and die). The discipline: normalize at the parse boundary, validate types, retry once per item.
**Sources:** comic-manga-narrator docs/DEVLOG.md sessions 2+5 (bugs 3, 6, 9); docs/LESSONS.md §1-2; backlog P-6, P-7.
**Targets:** linkedin, reddit:r/LocalLLaMA, hn

## [queued] the-bug-was-a-zero-size-stack
**Angle:** A Flutter game rendered pure blank — and three sophisticated theories (dual-GPU passthrough, the Flame engine, "X11 screenshots of GL animations lie") were all wrong. The fix was one line: a Stack sizes itself to its largest NON-positioned child, and a conditional `SizedBox.shrink()` overlay collapsed the whole game to 0×0 during play. What found it: replacing the subtree with a red ColoredBox and bisecting — plus a human eyewitness report that falsified the lying-screenshots theory. Debug by bisection, not theory; and when your verification tool might be lying, verify the verifier.
**Sources:** komorebi repo CHANGELOG 1.1.1 + commit 3ed3b9d (regression test); session memory 2026-06-12.
**Targets:** linkedin, reddit:r/FlutterDev, hn

## [queued] six-footguns-installing-a-local-ai-agent-on-ubuntu-24
**Angle:** Phase 2.5 of my Hermes install surfaced six environmental traps the spec never anticipated, each costing real time: (1) PEP 668 puts the `hermes` binary in `~/.local/bin` not `/usr/local/bin`, breaking every systemd `ExecStart`; (2) `pip install hermes[web]` doesn't actually install `aiohttp`; (3) the dashboard refuses `0.0.0.0` without `--insecure`; (4) nvm-managed Node is invisible to systemd (use NodeSource apt); (5) Workspace silently requires `HERMES_PASSWORD` when `HOST=0.0.0.0`; (6) the worst one — a profile-level `.env` with `override=True` silently REPLACED a strong random `API_SERVER_KEY` with a weak guessable value. Lesson: parameterize binary paths at install time, never trust extras-specs, and document config precedence aggressively.
**Sources:** sovereign doc Decision Log §v1.6.2-1 (the six + the four lessons); backlog H-12..H-17.
**Targets:** linkedin, reddit:r/selfhosted, hn

## [queued] my-tested-workflows-broke-from-upstream-drift
**Angle:** I ran a benchmark sweep across 9 ComfyUI workflows I'd built and "tested" weeks earlier. Result: only 5 of 9 still validate on the current ComfyUI — 4 broke from upstream drift. `ModelSamplingFlux.patch() got an unexpected keyword argument` (a node API changed under me), `Node 'Video Latent' has no class_type` (a custom node renamed/removed), Hunyuan workflows missing 4-5 nodes each. The honest lesson: "tested and working" has a SHELF LIFE on a fast-moving stack. A workflow is a snapshot against an environment that keeps moving; without pinned custom-node versions, your tested artifacts rot. Either pin everything, or treat re-validation as routine maintenance — and BENCHMARK PERIODICALLY so you discover the rot before a client does.
**Sources:** creative benchmarks COVERAGE.md sweep 2026-06-13; /tmp/sweep-results.csv; the 4 specific errors.
**Targets:** linkedin, reddit:r/comfyui, reddit:r/StableDiffusion, hn

## [queued] one-rtx-5090-how-many-users-the-honest-answer
**Angle:** "How many people can use a local LLM at once?" I stress-tested it instead of guessing. Sustained single-stream: 276 tok/s. Concurrency: 2 users=1.4x, 4=2.4x, KNEES at 8 concurrent (728 tok/s aggregate, flat after). vLLM continuous batching means throughput does NOT divide by user count, but KV-cache memory caps concurrent active generations at ~8 on 32GB. Translation: ~10-15 heavy or ~30-40 light chat users on one RTX 5090, latency under 2.5s.
**Sources:** sovereign nemotron-stress.csv; stress test 2026-06-13.
**Targets:** linkedin, reddit:r/LocalLLaMA, hn

## [queued] a-self-healing-catalogue-for-a-stack-that-drifts
**Angle:** I benchmarked 9 ComfyUI workflows and found 4 had silently broken from upstream node renames (HunyuanVideoModelLoader -> HunyuanVideo15*, EmptyWanLatentVideo -> Wan22ImageToVideoLatent). "Tested" rots. So I built a monthly maintenance job: it validates every workflow against the LIVE node registry, auto-discovers new ones, regenerates a public catalogue with real numbers, and pings my phone with what drifted — but deliberately does NOT auto-"fix" renames (different I/O = a human/LLM rebuild call, not a safe auto-patch). The honest engineering line: automate detection and reporting fully; gate the risky repairs behind judgment.
**Sources:** scripts/workflow-validate.py + generate-catalogue.py + workflow-maintenance.sh; COVERAGE.md; benchmarks.csv.
**Targets:** linkedin, reddit:r/comfyui, hn

## [queued] my-uncensored-llm-was-leaking-raw-tokenizer-output
**Angle:** Benchmarking my two unrestricted local LLMs (the creative-pipeline script writers): CPU Nemotron PRISM ran clean at 18 tok/s, no refusals. But GPU Dolphin 24B AWQ — same uncensored role — came back at ~2 tok/s with `Ġ` and `Ċ` BPE artifacts leaking into the output text. Classic symptom of a chat-template/decode misconfig at the serving layer (awq_marlin @ 0.25 GPU-mem). Lesson: "it responds" isn't "it works" — inspect the actual bytes, and benchmark every model you rely on, because a misconfigured serving config produces plausible-looking garbage.
**Sources:** COSTING.md encoder section (private numbers); /tmp/encoder-bench.py; 2026-06-14.
**Targets:** linkedin, reddit:r/LocalLLaMA

## [queued] i-built-an-ai-agent-company-on-my-own-hardware
**Angle:** Stood up a Paperclip "company" (CEO/Builder/Reviewer) on a fully local Hermes+LiteLLM stack. It works — agents autonomously wrote code, tests, and a CI pipeline. But three honest lessons: (1) ~40s per-run overhead forces a two-tier design (fast Hermes-direct vs governed Paperclip); (2) the cheap CEO self-reviewed and APPROVED genuinely buggy code (an auth-less health check that reports a live service as DOWN) — mocked tests hid it; (3) model choice dominates everything (DeepSeek-Flash 0.8s vs Kimi 27-40s). Takeaway: a strong independent reviewer + a permanent human merge gate is load-bearing, not optional.
**Sources:** EXECUTION-PLAN.md 2026-06-14 entries; queue §B PA-1..PA-9; /data/ai/08-portfolio/devkit.
**Targets:** linkedin, reddit:r/LocalLLaMA, hn

**L** = footnote material, fold into related posts.

## Hermes / LiteLLM / cockpit (H)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|

## Audio stack (A) — details in repo LESSONS.md
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A-1 | H | Entry points lie: 3 of 5 model repos had different real entry points than their README | LESSONS.md | queued (25-ways post) |
| A-2 | M | faster-whisper needs CTranslate2 conversion + preprocessor_config (n_mels silent corruption) | LESSONS.md | queued (25-ways) |
| A-3 | M | Fish Speech pinned to v1.5.1: code and weights must move together | LESSONS.md | queued (25-ways) |
| A-4 | M | Hallo2: six stacked fixes (xformers index, NCCL, GLES symlink, eager attn, output path, right repo org) | LESSONS.md | queued (25-ways) |
| A-5 | M | YuE: LFS pointer stubs from plain git clone; sdpa patch; mono MP3 output | LESSONS.md | queued (25-ways) |
| A-6 | M | RQ pattern: gateway enqueues, separate GPU worker consumes — and the worker needs the full nvidia runtime block | LESSONS.md | queued (25-ways) |
| A-7 | M | Unified workflow catalogue: one page now merges scriptwriting (Nemotron/Dolphin) + creative + audio; each tile carries BOTH a quality tier and a capability, with a client-side Group-by + filter toggle (default = tier). Driven by audio-benchmarks.csv with honest measured/estimate/perf-pending/blocked badges — only 3 audio pipelines have hard numbers, empty cells stay empty | generate-catalogue.py rewrite; audio-benchmarks.csv | unmined |
| A-8 | L | Status-from-notes detection must match an explicit uppercase `BLOCKED` marker, not a lowercased "block" substring — Vanisher's prose ("dep that blocked the video nodes") false-flagged the tile as blocked until the check was tightened | generate-catalogue.py status_of() | unmined |
| A-9 | H | The audio gateway's music worker pointed ACE-Step at a non-existent inference.py (that path holds the WEIGHTS, not the code); Stable Audio needs `stable_audio_tools` which requires Python<3.11 (worker is 3.12). Fix: ACE-Step runs in an ISOLATED venv (`--system-site-packages` reuses the SM_120 torch, deps isolated so YuE/Fish Speech untouched) → real **stereo 48kHz** instrumental in ~10s (1.8s diffusion). Also: Maestro's "ACE-Step" score was actually YuE 7B all along | ace-step-repo + ace-step-venv; workers/music.py | unmined |
| A-10 | H | ALL 3 local lip-sync models broken in the consolidated worker: LatentSync emits structurally corrupt output (mouth melt/seams; silent diffusers drift), Hallo2 hits a diffusers API break (`_set_gradient_checkpointing 'enable'`) + missing libGLESv2, MuseTalk blocked on mmcv/cu130. Root cause: one shared env can't satisfy models that need pinned isolated envs (they were separate containers originally). Local lip-sync = social-grade ceiling; broadcast = cloud. Rebuild target = MuseTalk 1.5 | musetalk-1.5-rebuild-spec.md | RESOLVED → A-11 (MuseTalk) / A-13 (Hallo2) / A-14 (LatentSync 1.6) — all 3 now rebuilt as dedicated pinned services |
| A-11 | H | MuseTalk 1.5 REBUILT & working (2026-06-21). The mmcv/SM_120 wall is unwinnable on RTX 5090: no `mmcv._ext` wheel for torch 2.8/cu128, and `mmcv-lite` can't satisfy mmpose (it imports EDPoseHead→`mmcv.ops` at import time). Fix: drop mmpose entirely and run the SAME `dw-ll_ucoco_384` DWPose model via **rtmlib + onnxruntime** — identical 133-keypoint output, zero mmcv. Packaged as a dedicated isolated image (`FROM mushishi-audio-base` + rtmlib) behind `creative-musetalk:9005`; the worker's `lipsync.py` museTalk branch HTTP-calls it like it calls creative-tts, so the YuE+Fish worker is never touched. Three more traps fixed: torch 2.6+ `weights_only=True` default rejects MuseTalk's legacy face-parse checkpoints; image inputs hit a `save_dir_full` NameError in inference.py; whisper-**tiny** (hidden=384, matches the PE) is mandatory — whisper-large is the wrong dim. Verified e2e through the gateway: 78s for a 34.5s 1024×1024 talking-head, ~7.7GB peak, VRAM freed on idle, social-grade (coherent mouth, no melt/smear). | musetalk-1.5-rebuild-spec.md; 06-configs/audio/Dockerfile.musetalk; museTalk/musetalk_server.py; preprocessing.py | unmined |
| A-12 | H | Cross-language dub = a hard 32GB VRAM wall, and a rebuild that bit itself (2026-06-22). Baking libgles2/torchsde into the worker image pulled unpinned `onnxruntime-gpu` to 1.27.0 (needs CUDA 13) on a CUDA-12.8 base → `libcudart.so.13` ImportError broke transcribe/dub; the ACE-Step smoke test missed it (no onnxruntime path). Pinned ==1.26.0. Then dub Option B (load GPU Nemotron per job, purge after): translation ROUTING works (dub.py→LiteLLM `sovereign-only`→:8000; `cooldown_time:0` so a per-job model isn't locked out; worker reaches the firewalled Nemotron only via LiteLLM), but the pipeline needs the ~26GB Nemotron + Whisper + Fish Speech AT ONCE (~31.7GB) — no co-load fits. SOLVED via a phased cross-dub.sh: one model at a time (transcribe→restart worker to free Whisper→load Nemotron→translate→purge→TTS→mux, ~29GB peak), validated EN→ES. Bonus: the GPU Nemotron is a reasoning model — needs detailed-thinking-off + big max_tokens or empty content. | gateway/workers/dub.py; scripts/cross-dub.sh; litellm config sovereign-only | unmined |
| A-13 | H | Hallo2 cinematic lip-sync rebuilt (2026-06-22), restoring the tier MuseTalk can't do (head pose + expression + CodeFormer) as its own pinned `mushishi-hallo2` image behind `creative-hallo2:9006`. Two lessons. (1) **The smallest correct pin beats the model's own lockfile.** The only real break is diffusers — Hallo2 ships its own UNet overriding `_set_gradient_checkpointing(module, value)` (old sig), so base diffusers 0.38 (`enable=`) raised `unexpected keyword 'enable'`; fix = pin `diffusers==0.32.2` ALONE. Porting Hallo2's whole requirements trio onto a newer base is actively wrong: `numpy==1.26.4` breaks the base's numpy-2-native scipy 1.18 (`np.long`, re-added in numpy 2.0) → insightface crash. (2) **A version trap can hide at the LAST step:** base `decorator 5.3.1` breaks moviepy 1.0.3's mp4 mux (`fps=None`) → pin `decorator==4.4.2`; the tell was every COMPUTE step passing and only file-WRITING failing → suspect an I/O lib, not the model. Cinematic is expensive: ~13.3GB, ~35× slower than realtime (1197s for a 34.5s 512² clip) vs MuseTalk's 78s — ~15× slower. Verified e2e (lipsync quality=cinematic→hallo2). | hallo2-rebuild-spec.md; 06-configs/audio/Dockerfile.hallo2; hallo2/hallo2_server.py; gateway/workers/lipsync.py | unmined |
| A-15 | M | **ComfyUI holds its models in VRAM after a generation — free them before any co-resident GPU job** (2026-06-22, lived while making the Hallo2 half-body sample). Generated a FLUX.1-dev half-body portrait via ComfyUI, then immediately ran a Hallo2 cinematic job through the gateway → `torch.OutOfMemoryError: ... 16.94 MiB is free`. Cause: ComfyUI cached the ~20GB FLUX model resident; Hallo2 (~13GB) had nowhere to load. Both fit the 32GB card *individually* — it's the co-residence that doesn't, the exact VRAM-sequencing lesson from the dub work (A-12). Fix: `POST http://localhost:8188/free {"unload_models":true,"free_memory":true}` to drop ComfyUI back to ~3GB baseline, then the job runs. Lesson: a model server that loads-on-demand does NOT necessarily free-on-idle the instant the *request* ends — verify `nvidia-smi` is actually back to baseline before launching the next stack's GPU job, don't assume. (Bonus: Hallo2 outputs 512² but PRESERVES a half-body input's framing — head+torso+gesture survive, so the cinematic tier's non-headshot differentiator shows even at 512².) | ComfyUI /free endpoint; gateway lipsync cinematic; benchmarks/samples/avatar-hallo2-halfbody.mp4 | unmined |
| A-14 | H | LatentSync 1.6 rebuilt as the **production** portrait tier (2026-06-22), closing the A-10 corruption. Three lessons. (1) **The same one-line pin fixed a THIRD model** — `diffusers==0.32.2` ALONE. LatentSync 1.5's "melt + affine seams" was never a tuning/margin bug; it was silent diffusers drift (base ≥0.32.0 → ~0.38) in the shared worker, the identical root cause as Hallo2 (A-13). Dedicated pinned image (`FROM mushishi-audio-base` + that one pin) behind `creative-latentsync:9007`; no decorator pin (LatentSync muxes via imageio not moviepy), no transformers pin (it uses its OWN bundled whisper). (2) **A "1.6" can be a pure checkpoint swap** — the ByteDance card says 1.6 is the SAME code/strategy as 1.5, only the 512² training set + checkpoint differ ("load the checkpoint, set resolution"). So the rebuild was: download the 1.6 `latentsync_unet.pt`, run on the existing `stage2_512.yaml` (already res=512), reuse 1.5's whisper-tiny + insightface buffalo_l (byte-identical) via the repo `checkpoints` symlink, pre-warm sd-vae-ft-mse into the shared HF cache. Result: **regression fully closed on BOTH portraits** that broke 1.5. (3) **Honest A/B can end in "kept anyway, not because it won."** The spec made this the one rebuild allowed to end in *drop it* — it had to be *visibly sharper than MuseTalk* on teeth/lips to earn its keep. It WASN'T: teeth/lip sharpness ≈ a tie, and it costs ~3× wall-clock (242s/34.5s) + ~2.6× VRAM (~20GB, not the doc's stale ~6GB est — 512² latent diffusion + VAE + DeepCache is heavy). Its real edge is cleaner lower-face/beard blending (no MuseTalk paste-box) + more natural skin; user kept it as the production quality tier for that, draft stays MuseTalk. The benchmark records the tie honestly. | latentsync-1.6-rebuild-spec.md; 06-configs/audio/Dockerfile.latentsync; latentsync/latentsync_server.py; gateway/workers/lipsync.py | unmined |

| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |

| I-1 | H | cron PATH ≠ shell PATH; monitor outcomes not exit codes; heartbeat the watchdog | post live | published 2026-06-10 |

| I-5 | M | Registrar parking DNS records block Cloudflare custom domains | 2026-06-11 | queued (one-evening) |
| I-6 | M | Zone-scoped CF tokens can't manage Workers/Pages (account-level perm) | same | queued (one-evening) |
| I-7 | M | Hetzner Storage Boxes ship with ALL access toggles off — looks like wrong password | same | queued (one-evening) |
| I-8 | L | restic sftp needs relative path on storage boxes (`:./repo` not `:/repo`) | same | queued (one-evening) |
| I-9 | L | Cloudflare Pages → Workers migration: new sites need wrangler.jsonc assets config | same | queued (one-evening) |
| I-10 | L | smartd on Ubuntu: enable error is an alias quirk; real unit is smartmontools.service | 2026-06-10 | unmined |
| I-11 | L | GitHub email-privacy rejects pushes with real email — use the noreply identity from commit #1 | same | unmined |
| I-12 | M | Backup coverage inventory question: "can I re-download this?" — everything answering no goes in | backup.sh history | unmined |
| I-13 | M | Docs referenced a script (agent-mode.sh) that never existed — phantom-reference drift | 2026-06-10 | unmined |
| I-14 | M | GUI mode-picker at login: zenity + autostart beats remembering mode scripts | mode-picker.sh | unmined |
| I-15 | H | Headless `claude -p` in cron died silently: OAuth access token expired with an EMPTY refresh token (can't self-renew) AND the CLI prints the 401 but EXITS 0 — so the generic "did not publish" alert hid the cause for days. Fix = long-lived `claude setup-token` in a dedicated env file (decouples cron from the browser-refresh OAuth) + a preflight auth probe + a content-based (not exit-code) auth-fail alert + a proactive credential-expiry check in the watchdog. The I-1 lesson ("monitor outcomes, not exit codes") recurring in a new disguise. Two follow-on traps when restoring: (a) a wrapped paste split the token across two lines → only a truncated prefix assigned → 401 + a bare-token "command not found" when sourced (tokens must be ONE line, no whitespace); (b) the CLI's persisted GLOBAL default model had drifted to the local LiteLLM alias `coding`, which a headless cloud run can't use → pin `--model` in the script, never trust the ambient default. | 2026-06-24 blog-bot incident; blog-bot.sh/healthcheck.sh | unmined |
| I-17 | H | **A safety gate that conflicts with a mandatory convention doesn't fail loud — it deadlocks FOREVER.** blog-bot refused to publish unless the tree was clean (its prompt ran `git add -A`, and push = live deploy), but the repo's own rule makes every session dirty pipeline/queue.md with lesson rows while pushing stays owner-gated — so the tree is dirty most of the time BY DESIGN, and the gate silently froze 21 queued posts for 5 days (blog incident #3, after OAuth-expiry and the catalogue-mirror dirtying). Gate and convention were each individually correct and jointly a deadlock. Fix = remove the gate's REASON, not the convention: publish stages an explicit pathspec (the post + its public/ assets + queue.md, whose rows ride along — public by convention), unrelated dirt is left alone and reported in a non-blocking INFO, and a post-push allowlist check alerts loudly if a commit ever sweeps out-of-pathspec files. GIT-1 ("commit with explicit pathspec in any repo you didn't just clean") applied to the bot that violated it. | 2026-07-02 blog-bot incident #3; blog-bot.sh + weekly-draft.md step 7 | unmined |

## Bench & headless-ComfyUI session (E, 2026-06-12)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|

## Benchmark sweep findings (2026-06-13)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BS-1 | H | 4 of 9 "tested" workflows broke from ComfyUI upstream drift (node API changes + missing custom nodes). Tested-and-working has a shelf life; pin node versions or re-validate as routine maintenance | sweep 2026-06-13 | queued (drift post) |
| BS-2 | M | Goldsmith measured: 6s/11.2GB for a 1024² keyframe (confirms the catalogue's ~3.3s ballpark, slower in practice) | sweep | unmined |
| BS-3 | M | graphToPrompt() validates a workflow's node availability headlessly without queueing — fast way to audit a whole workflow library for drift | sweep technique | unmined |
| BS-14 | H | Inkwell (Dolphin 24B AWQ) MEASURED 2026-06-22: 106 tok/s single-stream decode, GPU-resident, 32K ctx (catalogue est was ~80; flipped pending→measured, leaving Daybreak as the only estimate). | CRE-1 | unmined |
| BS-15 | H | The "raw tokenizer Ġ/Ċ leak" root cause finally nailed: the AWQ repo's `tokenizer_config.json` declares `tokenizer_class: LlamaTokenizer`, so transformers 5.x routes it through the broken Mistral-regex slow path and the byte-level BPE decoder never maps `Ġ`→space / `Ċ`→newline. The `tokenizer.json` itself is FINE (round-trips clean under `PreTrainedTokenizerFast`/raw `tokenizers`). Fix = serve a tokenizer-only overlay dir with `tokenizer_class` overridden to `PreTrainedTokenizerFast` via vLLM `--tokenizer` (weights stay read-only). Neither `--tokenizer-mode slow` nor `fix_mistral_regex=True` fixed the *decode*. The historic ~2 tok/s was a SEPARATE bug — `--cpu-offload-gb 10` — not the model. Two distinct traps wearing one symptom. | CRE-1 | queued (uncensored-llm-tokenizer post follow-up) |

## ComfyUI drift — ALL Wan + Hunyuan FIXED + render-verified (2026-06-15 marathon)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BS-7 | H | 7 drifted workflows fixed: Wan (t1/t5-distill render-verified), SeedVR2+VACE (baked deps), and ALL 3 Hunyuan15 RENDER-VERIFIED (dreamforge/quickening/sharpscale → 1080p). Method: official ComfyUI templates + a reusable litegraph→API converter (ui2api_render.py: auto-fix model names, prune-to-output) + render+visual-verify each. Downloaded ~11GB encoders (qwen_2.5_vl, byt5, sigclip, 1080p SR). Only the 2 LLM-orchestration ones remain. | marathon | queued (drift post — strong proof frames) |
| BS-8 | H | The cinematic LLM-orchestration pipeline was rebuilt as a HOST orchestrator (a small driver script), not an in-graph ComfyUI LLM node. Three reasons the in-graph LLM failed: (1) the creative-comfyui container CANNOT reach the sovereign CPU LLM at host :8001 (docker-bridge isolation — and widening :8001 to 0.0.0.0 just to satisfy a node would needlessly expand a local model's exposure, so declined); (2) a reasoning model emits empty `content` unless given ~900+ max_tokens (it spends the budget in `reasoning_content` first); (3) an in-graph blocking call to a slow CPU endpoint is fragile. Clean design: the LLM runs on :8001 as a host pre-step, injects the prompt, and the GPU never co-loads the LLM with the diffusion models. | cinematic-pipeline build | queued (sovereign-pipeline post) |
| BS-9 | H | Flux 2 Klein base-4b QUALITY CLIFF: sharp at 1024x1024 and 1024x576, but MELTS into a structureless painterly blob at 1280x720. The distilled 4B is resolution-sensitive >~1MP. Keep keyframes <=1MP and match the I2V aspect (1024x576 -> Wan I2V 832x480 = clean downscale). A degraded keyframe silently propagates: the I2V faithfully animated the mush, so frame 0 was already wrong — always visually check the KEYFRAME before blaming the video model. | render-verify | unmined |
| BS-10 | M | Two silent traps + one shortcut: (a) VHS_VideoCombine save_output=false writes to ComfyUI /temp, not the output mount — looks like "no output" though the render succeeded; override save_output=true. (b) the litegraph->API converter treated VHS dict-style widgets_values as a list (keys became values) — fixed to map dict widgets by field name. (c) you can free ComfyUI VRAM with POST /free {unload_models,free_memory} to run YuE in the same session — no container mode-switch needed for SEQUENTIAL GPU sharing. ACE-Step (diffusers format, no combined ckpt) + stable_audio (lib import fails) not ready; YuE is the working music engine. | cinematic-orchestrator build | unmined |
| BS-11 | M | Merging a video LoRA (WanVideoLoraSelect -> WanVideoModelLoader) into an fp8-quantized 14B model HANGS ComfyUI's single-threaded HTTP server during the merge — GPU stays idle, /system_stats returns 000, needs a container restart. Reproduced twice. Lesson: the fp8 LoRA-merge is the cost; and for image-to-video a LoRA is often unnecessary anyway — Wan I2V animates whatever is already in the keyframe, so steer with the keyframe + prompt and keep the plain proven graph. Also hardened the litegraph->API converter to RETRY on poll timeouts instead of crashing, so a transient HTTP block no longer loses an in-flight render. | I2V pipeline | unmined |
| BS-12 | H | Two cinematic-pipeline quality lessons. (1) IMAGE MODEL FIT: a general text-to-image model (small distilled Flux) cannot draw coherent HUMAN anatomy for a video keyframe — limbs/faces come out as a jumble, and an I2V model faithfully animates that jumble. The fix isn't more steps/res/LoRA; it's a domain-appropriate image checkpoint (a realistic SDXL finetune nailed anatomy + a crisp face in 4s). A strong keyframe + GENTLE motion then survives the I2V intact — no motion LoRA needed. Lesson: match the keyframe model to the subject domain; check the keyframe before blaming the video model. (2) MUSIC ENGINE FIT: a lyrics-to-song model (YuE) produces VOCALS singing the lyrics — wrong for a cinematic SCORE. ACE-Step (native ComfyUI, all-in-one ckpt) makes real INSTRUMENTAL music with empty lyrics in ~8s vs YuE's ~280s, and runs in-session (no audio-stack mode-switch). | cinematic-pipeline polish | unmined |

## KV-cache correction + catch-up lessons (2026-06-21)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S-18 | H | KV-cache math on a Mamba-hybrid: I costed Nemotron's FP8 KV as a dense transformer (×52 layers) and claimed "~500 MiB = ~25K ctx tokens" — wrong by ~14×. NemotronH is a Mamba-2/Transformer-MoE hybrid; only 6 of 52 layers attend, so attention-KV ≈ 3 KB/token (= 2×6×2×128) ≈ ~350K tokens/GiB. Bigger correction: attention-KV is NOT the context bottleneck on a hybrid — 32GB is dominated by weights + CUDA graphs + multimodal buffers + per-sequence Mamba state. 256K = max_position_embeddings (theoretical); ~228K = total-VRAM ceiling (NOT a KV ceiling). Lesson: cost KV against the ACTUAL architecture. | sovereign §7/§8/§13 + README; corrected a-32gb-budget post | published 2026-06-21 (i-had-my-kv-cache-math-14x-wrong) |
| I-15 | M | Silent-cron-miss catch-up: when a scheduled job is found to have been silently not-running, don't just fix it — do the catch-up pass for everything it should have produced/checked while dead, and reconcile the gap. A fix that resumes the schedule but ignores the missed window leaves stale state behind. | backup/watchdog 17-day arc follow-through | unmined |
| PA-14 | H | Capable agents route AROUND prompt-level constraints: a prompt saying "you are a coordinator, never implement" does not stop a tool-capable agent from implementing — it self-serves because it CAN. Control must be STRUCTURAL (remove the file-write/code tools; route task-types to specific agents), not instructional. Generalises beyond Paperclip: any guardrail you can phrase as a sentence, a capable model can ignore. | Paperclip PA-11/PA-12 generalised | unmined |
| BS-13 | M | I2V chaining drift: chaining image-to-video segments accumulates drift — each segment animates the previous frame's imperfections, so error compounds across a multi-clip sequence (color/identity/structure wander). Steer hard at each keyframe and re-anchor rather than trusting a long autoregressive I2V chain to stay on-model. | cinematic-pipeline I2V observation | unmined |
| I-16 | H | Notifications are blind when the job never runs: outcome-monitoring (snapshot-age, ntfy alerts) catches a job that runs-and-fails, but a job that NEVER FIRES produces no log line to alert on — silence reads as "healthy." Heartbeat the SCHEDULER itself (expect a positive "ran today" signal), not just the job's output, or a dead cron entry is invisible. Extends I-1's heartbeat lesson to the un-run case. | watchdog design follow-up | unmined |
| SA-2 | M | SaaS "I-wish-there-was" search bias: mining "I wish there was an app for X" / "is there a tool that…" posts to find SaaS ideas selects for problems people can ARTICULATE and that sound buildable — a survivorship/articulation bias that over-weights crowded, already-served niches and under-weights painful-but-unsexy workflows nobody phrases as a wish. Validate demand against revealed behaviour (what people already hack together / pay for), not stated wishes. | SaaS-idea pipeline research | unmined |
| OTEL-1 | M | "Tracing is broken because the SDK is missing" was the wrong diagnosis — the LiteLLM image already bundled the OTEL SDK+exporter and the callbacks initialized fine; the real failure was the EXPORTER TARGET. Two compounding traps: (1) this Phoenix build serves its OTLP collector over HTTP on its UI port (:6006/v1/traces, advertised in Phoenix's own startup log) — the published :4318 was never bound and gRPC :4317 only accepts the TCP handshake, so "the port is open" is not "the collector is here"; (2) LiteLLM's `_normalize_otel_endpoint` appends `/v1/traces` even to the gRPC exporter target, which is an HTTP-only convention, silently corrupting the gRPC channel. Lesson: when traces don't land, verify WHERE the collector actually listens (read the server's own log line, send one probe span) before touching the client image — and prefer the transport the server advertises. | LiteLLM→Phoenix OTEL debug (AI-5) | unmined |

## Hermes update — dual-install topology (2026-06-22)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| HM-1 | M | "Update Hermes" is ambiguous because THREE checkouts/installs drift independently: (a) pip CLI `~/.local/bin/hermes` (system py3.12 user-site — upgrade with `pip install --user --upgrade --break-system-packages hermes-agent`; plain `uv pip` targets a SEPARATE 3.11 env and won't touch the live binary); (b) the git gateway install `~/.hermes/hermes-agent` w/ its own venv that runs the :8642 gateway — upgrade with `hermes update` (git pull + dep reinstall + auto gateway restart); (c) the desktop source `/data/ai/hermes-desktop-src` you actually launch. Map which install BEFORE "updating," and check both agent versions (they were drifted 0.16.0 vs 0.17.0). | hermes 0.16→0.17 (2026.6.5→2026.6.19) update | unmined |
| HM-2 | M | `hermes update` silently builds a SECOND desktop bundle inside its own checkout (`~/.hermes/hermes-agent/apps/desktop/release/linux-unpacked/Hermes`) at main-tip — divergent from the release-pinned `/data/ai/hermes-desktop-src` build the running app launches from. A self-update can leave you with two desktop binaries at different versions; pick one as canonical or they confuse "which Hermes am I running." Desktop rebuild = `npm ci` at repo root (warm cache ~8s) then `cd apps/desktop && npm run pack` (--dir → linux-unpacked); verify via the launcher's `install stamp:` line matching the target commit. | observed during update | unmined |

| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| QI-1 | H | HuggingFace `hf download` crashes on large fp8 safetensors via the **Xet** backend (`RuntimeError: Internal Writer Error: Background writer channel closed`) — connectivity is fine (the resolve URL 302s to cas-bridge.xethub.hf.co which serves the bytes). | QI-2 | M | grep the existing model dirs BEFORE downloading any multi-GB component. The Qwen2.5-VL text encoder already existed at `/data/ai/02-models/clip/split_files/text_encoders/qwen_2.5_vl_7b_fp8_scaled.safetensors` (byte-identical) — only the 20GB DiT was actually missing. A blind download created a 9.4GB duplicate at `clip/`. CLEANUP (next creative-mode session, ComfyUI up): repoint orchestrator `render_keyframe_qwen` clip_name to the `split_files/text_encoders/...` path, delete the `clip/` dup, re-verify a render. | Qwen-Image install | open-cleanup |
| QI-3 | H | Qwen-Image (20B fp8, **Apache-2.0**) is the genuinely commercial-safe image hero — promoted to the `--mode commercial` DEFAULT (replacing the interim chroma pick that followed the FLUX.1-dev non-commercial discovery; chroma stays as `--keyframe chroma` artistic alt; ledger/FLUX.1-dev = explicit non-commercial drafts only). Measured: 12s warm / 16-20s cold, ~28GB peak (DiT 20.4GB + text-enc 9.4GB, fits 32GB w/ ~4GB headroom), eye-verified 3 seeds. ComfyUI graph: UNETLoader + CLIPLoader(type=qwen_image) + VAELoader + ModelSamplingAuraFlow(shift 3.1) + KSampler euler/simple 20 steps cfg 2.5. Catalogue tile = "Sterling". | CRE Qwen-Image wiring + benchmark | unmined |
| QI-4 | M | Coding-stack re-stand = mode switch, not co-load: `source gpu-tenants.sh; stop_all_gpu_tenants` (frees ComfyUI's resident VRAM) then `cd /data/ai/06-configs/vllm-coding && docker compose up -d`. `:8000` is exclusive (only ONE vLLM tenant at a time); healthy in ~35s; structured `tool_calls` verified via LiteLLM `:4000` model=coding (qwen3_coder parser). `:4000` needs the `LITELLM_MASTER_KEY` bearer. restart=unless-stopped = persistent. | AI-1 coding stack re-stand | unmined |
