Reliability-critical public-repo pushes are arguably safer main-loop-sequential than via a fresh agent fleet that could re-trip the limit mid-push. | qwen workflow session-limit recovery | unmined |

## Private catalogue: generated + locally hosted; auteur reconcile (2026-06-22)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PC-1 | M | A derived/private view must be GENERATED from the canonical source, not hand-copied. The private catalogue was a manual copy of the public one and went stale within hours (missed Sterling, kept the wrong Ledger licence). Fix: a generator that re-runs the upstream public generator + lifts its DATA verbatim, then appends the private-only tiles — so it always mirrors live. Host local-only views on 127.0.0.1 via a `systemd --user` unit + `enable-linger` (survives reboot, no login), never tailnet/public; a daily user-timer keeps it regenerated. | private-catalogue generator + localhost host | unmined |
| PC-2 | H | Catalogue honesty — a hand-written tile claimed a capability the code deliberately does NOT run: the auteur/uncensored I2V path's add-on LoRAs hang the renderer's HTTP during the fp8 merge (reproducible), so the orchestrator animates the keyframe with the plain graph instead. A description written by hand drifts from what the pipeline executes; verify every tile against the actual code path before quoting it as a capability. | auteur I2V LoRA reconcile (BIZ-1) | unmined |
| PC-3 | M | cinematic-orchestrator `--stage all` scores via native ACE-Step in ComfyUI (~8s), NOT the separate YuE worker path that also exists in the file (`render_music`). When two code paths implement the same stage, confirm which one the entry point actually calls before quoting a wall-clock — the auteur end-to-end is 669s (ACE-Step), not the ~767s an older YuE-assumption implied. | auteur end-to-end measure | unmined |

## theinvalid.me design review — Stitch export (2026-06-22)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SITE-1 | M | An AI design tool (Google Stitch) regenerated the site as a "Technical Terminal" system — and nearly every "upgrade" it proposed broke a DELIBERATE decision: Tailwind-CDN runtime JS (vs zero-JS), 4 Google webfonts (vs system-fonts-only / "performance IS the flex"), a 2nd co-equal green accent (vs one gold), navy bg (vs near-black). Verdict: design-tool exports are inspiration for LAYOUT/CONTENT, never drop-in code — the generator optimises for "looks designed," not for the locked constraints that ARE the brand. Mine the structure, reject the chrome. Post angle: "An AI redrew my portfolio and told me more about my constraints than my design." | ~/Documents/design/theinvalid-stitch-export/; reference_theinvalid_stitch_design memory; SITE-DECISIONS.md | unmined |
| SITE-2 | M | The one genuinely good idea from that export: its "Local LLM Field Guide" structure (the math nobody tells you → memory tier map → what runs where → interactive picker, framed "without the benchmarketing"). Captured as an in-brand `/calculator` upgrade spec — but re-based off Stitch's Apple-Silicon unified-memory / generic "70% rule" / invented model names onto our MEASURED dedicated-GPU reality (the 14× KV-cache correction, the 32GB budget, the how-many-users concurrency knee). The structure travels; the domain + numbers must be ours. | docs/SPEC-llm-field-guide.md; posts i-had-my-kv-cache-math-14x-wrong + a-32gb-gpu-is-a-budget + one-rtx-5090-how-many-users | unmined |
| SITE-3 | M | IA consolidated 8 nav tabs → 5 (2026-06-23): `start here · work with me · projects · field guide · knowledge base`. Two root causes of tab-bloat: the homepage already duplicates projects+blog, and the nav exposed BOTH audiences' full journeys flat (buyers: start-here→services→case-studies→resume; engineers: projects→blog→calculator→github). Merges: case studies → a pinned section in Knowledge base (/blog); `cloud vs local` calculator → renamed "field guide" (own tab, per owner); resume → OFF the nav (page kept, linked from work-with-me + a dated auto-current download `Name-Resume-YYYY-MM.pdf`); github → footer logo+username. Redirects `/case-studies` + `/knowledge-base` → `/blog`. Lesson: "too many tabs" was really two audience funnels exposed as siblings — collapse each funnel to one strong page, let the homepage be the hub. Phase 2 pending: build-time GitHub status sync (cards show "updated X ago") + resume PDF auto-gen + scheduled rebuild. | Base.astro nav/footer; blog/index.astro; astro.config redirects; resume.astro live-projects; SITE-DECISIONS (to update) | unmined |
| SITE-4 | L | TODO (owner, later): the auto-generated resume PDF is **4 pages** — a touch long. Trim `src/content/resume.md` for a tighter 1–2 page CV; the PDF tracks it automatically now (rendered at build by the daily-refresh workflow). Owner content call on what to cut. | src/content/resume.md; scripts/render-resume-pdf.mjs | todo |
| SITE-5 | H | The workflow catalogue **silently drifted from its own data source** (2026-06-24). `generate-catalogue.py` builds tiles from a hand-maintained `META` dict, but the workflow set actually lives in the benchmark CSVs — so when Qwen-Image (Sterling, the Apache-2.0 commercial image hero) landed in the CSV on 06-22, it never became a tile (META wasn't updated), the count showed 28 not 29, and the **monthly job auto-published the incomplete page with the generator's output suppressed** (`>/dev/null 2>&1`) → zero alert. Same audit caught a FALSE license claim: Ledger/FLUX.1-dev was labelled "Apache-2.0 commercial-safe" but FLUX.1-dev is **non-commercial**. Fix: added Sterling (→29 tiles), corrected Ledger (points to Sterling for paid work), added an explicit `IGNORE` set for the 11 benchmark-only CSV rows (superseded/runs/infra/aggregates), and a **`--strict` reconciliation guard** — every CSV workflow must be a tile or explicitly ignored, else the generator exits non-zero. Wired `workflow-maintenance.sh` to run `--strict`, **gate the auto-push on it**, and ntfy-alert instead of shipping incomplete. Lesson: a catalogue whose item-list is maintained *separately* from its data source WILL drift silently — reconcile the two and make the mismatch loud (fail the publish), or new work just vanishes from the shop window. | generate-catalogue.py (META+IGNORE+--strict); workflow-maintenance.sh; public/workflow-catalogue.html | unmined |
| SITE-6 | H | `status_of()` **downgraded a MEASURED workflow to "estimate" by substring-matching free text** (2026-06-25). Daybreak's CSV note reads `MEASURED 2026-06-22 (was estimate)` — the heuristic did `"estimate" in notes.lower()`, matched the word describing its PAST state, and flipped a measured pipeline to estimate. Same bug-class as A-8 (the BLOCKED prose-substring) — fixed there, missed here. Worse, a catalogue regen then **published the wrong "27 measured" count without anyone verifying it against the recorded data**. Fix: `status_of` now honors explicit UPPERCASE markers first (`MEASURED`/`ESTIMATE`/`BLOCKED`), then the wall-clock value (`~`-prefix = estimate), never the lowercase word. Same audit found Inkwell's catalogue static stale (`pending`/`~80 est`) vs the recorded BS-14 measurement (106 tok/s, "flipped pending→measured") — updated. Honest count restored to 29/29 measured. Lesson: a status DERIVED by substring-matching prose will misread text that mentions the very word it keys on; key on explicit markers, and NEVER publish a derived status without checking it against the recorded measurement. | generate-catalogue.py status_of() + Inkwell static; benchmarks.csv DailyPipeline row; queue BS-14 | unmined |

## Firecrawl cost control → self-hosted Crawl4AI (2026-06-23)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SCR-1 | M | Capping a metered scraping API (Firecrawl) without losing capability: keep it on the FREE tier (1000 credits/mo, no rollover) reserved ONLY for hard anti-bot/Cloudflare sites, offload the bulk to self-hosted Crawl4AI (free) with LLM-extraction routed through your own LiteLLM. The non-obvious split the "just self-host" advice misses: Crawl4AI replaces the SCRAPE, not the SEARCH — discovery still needs SearXNG (sovereign) or a search API, and most credit burn was search, not scrapes. Post angle: "I researched Firecrawl alternatives without spending a single Firecrawl credit." | crawl4ai deploy /data/ai/06-configs/crawl4ai/; feedback_scraping_stack memory | unmined |
| SCR-2 | M | Two Crawl4AI self-host gotchas: (1) its gunicorn binds 127.0.0.1 INSIDE the container, so a bridged `ports:` forward gets "connection reset" while the in-container healthcheck shows green — fix with `network_mode: host`. (2) On a loopback bind with no token it silently MINTS AN EPHEMERAL token each restart and returns "Authentication required" everywhere — set a stable `CRAWL4AI_API_TOKEN` and pass `Authorization: Bearer` on every call incl. the MCP `--header`. Read the server's auth code to find the real condition instead of guessing config flags. | server.py auth_gate; crawl4ai 0.9.0 | unmined |
| SCR-3 | L | Cheapest scraping optimization needs no new infra: prefer the FREE built-in WebSearch/WebFetch before any paid scraper; call the API's own refund hook (firecrawl_search_feedback refunds 1 credit/search); hit native sources (raw.githubusercontent.com, sitemaps, RSS) before rendering a page. Datacenter-IP self-hosting (VPS) is WORSE for anti-bot than a residential IP — don't reach for it as an IP-rotation trick. | this session's usage pattern | unmined |

## On-brand architecture diagrams across every repo (2026-06-23)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DIAG-1 | M | Mermaid vs drawio vs SVG isn't really the question — match the format to the READER. For a visual learner, a polished on-brand SVG (dark canvas, ONE gold accent, system fonts, gold=local/hero/ports + muted-dashed=cloud/fallback) conveys an architecture at a glance where Mermaid's auto-layout makes them read box-by-box and lose the thread. Hand-authored SVG committed to each repo's `docs/` + embedded via `<img>` renders on GitHub AND stays the source of truth. Swept all 3 stacks + comic + devkit + legal-rag + n8n + the infra topology + komorebi data-model onto one brand. Post angle: "I redrew every repo's architecture in my own brand — and learned the format question is really a reader question." | this session; feedback_diagram_brand memory; ~/Documents/diagrams/ | unmined |
| GIT-1 | H | `git add <my files>` then `git commit` commits the WHOLE INDEX, not just your files — if the repo already had OTHER files staged (komorebi had 25 pre-staged Play Store assets), your "add a diagram" commit silently bundles + pushes them with a misleading message. Fix: commit with an explicit pathspec — `git commit -- README.md docs/x.svg` — which commits ONLY those paths and leaves everything else staged. Recovery if already pushed: `git reset --soft HEAD~1` → pathspec re-commit → `git push --force-with-lease`. Always `git diff --cached --name-only` before committing in a repo you didn't just clean. | komorebi mis-commit 30d1fd8 → fixed 28f8bf9 | unmined |

## Mushishi mode-picker: idle-vs-loaded probe + missing Coding mode (2026-06-24)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MODE-1 | M | A GPU mode-switcher must distinguish a model that is merely RESIDENT (loaded, holding 29GB, doing nothing → safe to purge) from one that is WORKING (serving a request / running a render). The old picker conflated them: its guard flagged any >2GB GPU process, so it nagged "you'll kill running work" on EVERY switch even when the card was idle. Fix = an activity probe (`gpu_busy_report`/`gpu_resident_line` in gpu-tenants.sh) that reads the AUTHORITATIVE signal per workload — vLLM `:8000/metrics` `num_requests_running`+`_waiting`, ComfyUI `/queue`, audio RQ registries — with a GPU SM-util sample (`nvidia-smi --query-gpu=utilization.gpu`, peak over ~1.4s, >15% = busy) as a backstop ONLY when the exact probes say idle. "0% util in one nvidia-smi snapshot" is not proof (decode is bursty); sample a window AND check the request counters. The picker now shows `Loaded: <container · served-model>` + `Status: ✓ IDLE / ⚠ BUSY` in the header and only prompts to confirm on real work. Post angle: "my GPU said 29GB used, 0% busy — teaching a tool to tell 'loaded' from 'working'." | /data/ai/01-workspace/scripts/{gpu-tenants.sh,mode-picker.sh}; feedback_mode_gpu_stoplist memory | unmined |
| MODE-2 | M | A stop-list entry is not a start path. `vllm-coding` (Qwen3-Coder-30B-AWQ, the persistent LiteLLM `coding` backend) was in `GPU_TENANTS` so every mode EVICTED it, but there was no `coding-mode.sh` to BRING IT UP and it was absent from the picker — you could only stop it, never start it from the UI. Added `coding-mode.sh` (mirrors forensic/agent: `stop_gpu_tenants_except vllm-coding` → `docker compose up -d` → wait on `/v1/models`) and a "Coding" picker entry; also surfaced the already-existing-but-hidden `agent-mode.sh` (the picker's single "AI" row only ran forensic) and relabelled AI→Forensic. Bonus: stopping Coding (any switch / StopAll) drops :8000 and LiteLLM fails `coding` over to the always-on CPU Qwen coder :8002 — the CPU floor doubles as the mode-switch failover, so the CLI never loses its endpoint. | coding-mode.sh; vllm-coding/README.md; SPEC-coding-stack.md | unmined |

## Mushishi Mode — design-tool mockup → native GTK4 app, brand-reconciled (2026-06-24)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GUI-1 | M | A design-tool mockup (Stitch) is a STRUCTURE brief, not a skin to import — same rule as the site export (SITE-1). Stitch redrew the GPU switcher beautifully but in navy `#0b1326` + dual amber/green accent + 3 webfonts; we kept its LAYOUT (sidebar, status header, VRAM meter, mode rows w/ chips+sizes, footer) and reconciled the palette to the locked brand by porting the token system already realized in the Sovereign Legal RAG UI (`static/index.html`): near-black `#0A0D13`, ONE gold `#E8A33D` hero, emerald `#46B98A` STATUS-ONLY, mono labels. The reusable asset wasn't the mockup — it was a *sibling app's* CSS tokens. "Mine the structure, reject the chrome," now proven twice. | /data/ai/01-workspace/mushishi-mode/{DESIGN.md,style.css}; sovereign-legal-rag/static/index.html | unmined |
| GUI-2 | H | Python `str.splitlines()` splits on `\x1c`/`\x1d`/`\x1e` (FS/GS/RS), NOT just `\n`. A bash→Python IPC that joined fields with `\x1d` (Group Separator) had every record silently shredded — the GUI showed "no compute processes" while 28GB was in use, and `bash probe.sh` alone looked fine (the bug only appears once Python `.splitlines()` touches it). `\x1f` (Unit Separator) is SAFE (not a splitlines boundary); use `\x1f` for records + TAB for fields, never `\x1c/\x1d/\x1e`. Caught only by live screenshot verification — the unit looked correct. Post angle: "the ASCII separator that Python silently treats as a newline." | mushishi-mode/probe.sh + mushishi_mode.py `_apply_probe` | unmined |
| GUI-3 | M | Porting a SYNCHRONOUS guard to an ASYNC UI can silently weaken it. The zenity picker probed busy-state synchronously right before deciding; the GTK port read a cached value refreshed every 4s + empty at startup → it could skip the "you'll kill running work" confirmation. Fix: on Apply, re-probe and decide from the callback (fresh verdict), disable the action until the first probe lands. When you move a check off the critical path for responsiveness, re-establish freshness at the decision point. Also: a GTK `DrawingArea`+Cairo draw func needs `python3-gi-cairo` (pycairo↔GObject bridge) or it throws "foreign struct converter for cairo.Context" — a CSS-styled `Gtk.ProgressBar` avoids the dependency. | mushishi-mode/mushishi_mode.py; review wf_7323bc7a | unmined |
| GUI-4 | L | Adversarial multi-lens review (correctness/etiquette · brand · a11y · robustness) of a ~450-line GTK app surfaced a real high-sev bug (the splitlines one) plus AA-contrast (`ink3` 4.06:1<4.5), an unguarded destructive StopAll, and N/A-field parse fragility that a single self-review missed. For a narrow-solution-space build (given design + reference), the value is in VERIFYING, not re-generating — build once, adversarially verify, fix. | review workflow wf_7323bc7a; DESIGN.md v1.0.1 changelog | unmined |

## Tailscale ACL + Cloudflare AI-bots (2026-06-24)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NET-1 | H | Tailscale **tag-based ACLs are inert until the devices are tagged**. Applying the policy alone (tagOwners + tag-scoped accept rules) with untagged devices = every rule matches nothing = silent default-deny everywhere. "Apply the ACL" MUST include assigning each device its tag (admin console → Edit ACL tags, or `POST /api/v2/device/{id}/tags`). Symptom of the trap: even the explicitly-allowed ports are blocked while `tailscale ping` still works (ping is path-level, not ACL-gated). | BIZ-3 apply | unmined |
| NET-2 | M | Verify a directional ACL from the RESTRICTED side against the TARGET's tailnet IP — not the tester's own. Easy footgun: running `nc <my-own-tailnet-ip> <port>` tests the box against itself (its own sshd open, nothing else), proving nothing about the peer→peer rule. Always nc the *other* device's IP. | BIZ-3 verify footgun | unmined |
| NET-3 | M | Cloudflare's **Bot Management / rulesets / zone-settings APIs are paid-plan only** — on a Free zone, even a correctly-scoped Bot-Management token returns `9109`/`10000` "Unauthorized". The free "Block AI bots" feature is **dashboard-only**; there's no API to read/flip it on Free. Check actual bot access by behavior (curl with crawler UAs → 200/403) + robots.txt, not the API. | BIZ-12 | unmined |

## smartd NVMe self-tests + ntfy alerts (2026-06-24)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DSK-1 | M | smartmontools default `DEVICESCAN -d removable` still *health-monitors* fixed NVMe drives (smartd reports "Monitoring N NVMe"), but runs **NO scheduled self-tests** and mails local root — on a box with no MTA that's a SILENT safety net. For real coverage add explicit per-device lines with `-s (S/../.././02|L/../../6/03)` (short daily/long weekly). Don't assume "smartd is running" = "drives are self-tested + you'll be told." | BIZ-5 | unmined |
| DSK-2 | L | NVMe self-test (DST) support often isn't shown by `smartctl -c \| grep -i self-test` (ATA-phrased); detect it by probing `smartctl -t short` and checking the selftest log, or just trust modern NVMe (990 PRO supports it). And for alert delivery without a mailer, use smartd `-m <nomailer> -M exec <script>` to run only the exec hook (e.g. curl→ntfy). | BIZ-5 | unmined |

## Blog resilience: Claude-primary -> local-draft fallback -> Claude-reviews-on-return (2026-06-24)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BF-1 | H | Blog now degrades gracefully: when headless Claude can't run, blog-fallback.sh has a LOCAL model DRAFT the next [queued] slot into gitignored drafts/ (never auto-published, never dirties the publish tree), records it pending, and alerts. When Claude is back, blog-bot.sh's "review-on-return" has Claude review the draft, list concrete fixes, and alert — owner publishes after. Continuity (never lose a slot to an outage) WITHOUT shipping unreviewed local prose to a live client-facing site. Verified e2e: local draft 681 words/~100s; Claude review caught real technical errors. | blog Claude->local fallback build | unmined |
| BF-2 | M | Two traps when wiring a local LLM fallback for slow CPU long-form: (1) DON'T route it through LiteLLM's sovereign/failover aliases — their short timeouts (30/120s, tuned for fast GPU-failover) return HTTP 500 on a multi-minute CPU generation (and chain-compound through each fallback). Hit the always-on CPU endpoint DIRECTLY (own timeout). (2) DON'T use a REASONING model (nemotron-cpu-prism :8001) for drafting — it spends the entire max_tokens budget on the <think> block (5136 chars reasoning, 0 content, finish=length). Use a NON-reasoning model (qwen-coder-cpu :8002) — finishes naturally with clean markdown. | blog-fallback model/endpoint debug | unmined |

## Derived-artifact generators must not dirty a publish-gated repo + Hermes kanban visibility (2026-06-24)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BF-3 | H | A generator for a DERIVED artifact must be READ-ONLY w.r.t. any repo guarded by a clean-tree publish gate. The private-catalogue generator re-ran `generate-catalogue.py`, which rewrote `public/workflow-catalogue.html` (even just the "Updated <date>" line) → dirtied the theinvalid-site repo → `blog-bot.sh`'s clean-tree guard would `skip publish` EVERY day the regen timer fired (silent blog stall). Fix: the private mirror now READS the deployed public file, never regenerates it in place. Generalizes: any cron/daily job that writes into a `push=live` repo can silently wedge that repo's publish automation — keep generated state out of the guarded tree (or restore it after). | private-catalogue regen blocking blog | unmined |
| BF-4 | L | Hermes kanban doubles as a visibility surface for headless automation: `hermes kanban create "<title>" --body … --created-by <bot> --idempotency-key <k> --json` posts a card to the `default` board (shows on the personal Hermes desktop); leave `--assignee` UNSET so no dispatcher auto-runs it (it's a marker, not a task). Close the loop later with `hermes kanban comment <id>`. The blog fallback uses this: card on draft, Claude's review as a comment on return. | blog fallback → Hermes kanban | unmined |

## SovereignSec code auditor (SC) — local agentic code-security auditor (2026-06-25)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SC-1 | H | **The fine-tune added zero detection — and that's the result.** On a leak-resistant POST-CUTOFF eval (CVEs published 2025–26, fixes after the data cutoff) base Qwen2.5-Coder-32B and the fine-tuned 32B scored identical P/R/F1 1.0 → detection delta EXACTLY 0.0 (held-out 7B = +0.05 recall, noise). 879 real mined pairs + three runs (1.5B/7B/32B, loss 0.856/0.686/0.562) bought OUTPUT DISCIPLINE — one terse parseable calibrated `FINDING:{cwe,confidence}` line vs the base's five verbose formats — not new sight. Capability lives in the SYSTEM. Full hand-written draft already exists (this session). | SovereignSec-AI bench/results POSTCUTOFF_ABLATION + INSIGHTS + docs/FAQ | unmined |
| SC-2 | H | **My benchmark lied four times.** A toy eval (1 vuln fn + 1 safe fn) scored 1.0; the SAME system on real CVE files (full Django modules, ~14 fns) scored 0.07 — the toy measured "tell two hand-picked snippets apart," not detection. Worse, early "wins" were GRADER bugs: a detector matched the substring `secure` inside `insecure`; a format mismatch credited a finding for the wrong reason. Took 4 detector iterations + reading RAW model outputs to see it. Same bug-class as A-8 / SITE-6 (status-from-substring). The way you measure can lie louder than the thing you measure. | bench eval-harness fixes (BLOG E5/E6/E7); INSIGHTS | unmined |
| SC-3 | H | **The license picked the architecture.** CodeQL's licence forbids scanning private/customer code without a paid enterprise seat — dead on arrival for an air-gapped product whose whole point is private code. Semgrep's CROSS-FILE taint (the capability needed most) is paid-tier; the OSS engine is single-file. So the cross-file taint engine had to be hand-built on tree-sitter + a call graph. The constraint that looked like a limit became the moat: a cross-file engine I own, runs offline, no licence can revoke. | IMPL_SPEC licensing; INSIGHTS | unmined |
| SC-4 | H | **Naive SAST hints make a good LLM WORSE; the hybrid is a UNION, not a pipeline.** Feeding the 32B one-shot SAST hints dropped it 0.90→0.79 (anchoring). Agentic-alone is precise but blind beyond its rules (0.69 < bare 0.90). What wins runs the LLM (breadth) and the deterministic system (precision + proof) INDEPENDENTLY and MERGES them: hybrid 0.97. Neither pure-LLM nor pure-system wins. | bench HAYSTACK_REPORT_v2/v2cov; INSIGHTS #8; BLOG E9 | unmined |
| SC-5 | M | **Deterministic taint beats the LLM on cross-file precision.** On a benchmark seeded with safe-but-suspicious decoys (same sink, constant/local input), the cross-file taint engine = 1.00 precision / 1.00 recall; the per-file LLM = 0.25 precision / 0.60 recall (misses non-obvious cross-file vulns AND false-positives on the decoys). The engine flags exactly the user-reachable sinks, with a proof path. | bench CROSSFILE_PRECISION_REPORT | unmined |
| SC-6 | M | **A rule system is exactly as good as its coverage — measured before/after.** One-line fix — register `request.get_json()` as a taint SOURCE (the Flask JSON-body pattern an entire vuln class used) — lifted SSRF coverage 0→1.0; broadening rules moved agentic 0.41→0.69 and hybrid 0.93→0.97, proof-carrying findings 17→38. The per-class gaps left (path-traversal ~0.4, deserialization 0.2) point at exactly what to build next. | bench HAYSTACK_REPORT_v2cov; BLOG E10 | unmined |
| SC-7 | H | **A pseudonymous public repo leaked my real name via GIT METADATA, not content.** Creating the repo I set `git config user.name "<real legal name>"`, so every commit's author exposed the identity behind the pseudonym via `git log` — though NO content file held the name (the sanitization pass had scrubbed contents). The commit `+0800` offset was a location signal too. An adversarial pre-publish audit caught it AFTER the first push. Fix: pseudonym + GitHub noreply email + `TZ=UTC`, `git commit --amend --reset-author`, force-push; verify `git log --format='%an %ae %ai'` BEFORE pushing. Sanitize metadata, not just files. Same "guardrail must be structural" family as I-11. | SovereignSec-AI publish; pre-publish audit workflow | unmined |
| SC-8 | M | **The data moat: GHSA tags fix commits as `references[].type=="WEB"` NOT `"FIX"`** — harvest BOTH or the miner finds ~nothing (real run: 355 WEB / 0 FIX; FIX-only = 0 pairs, fixed code = 134). No off-the-shelf label-verified Python-web vuln set exists, so curated GHSA→fix-commit mining is the moat: 879 real vuln→patch pairs. Beware label noise (BigVul 68%F1 → PrimeVul 3%F1) and benchmark contamination. | ft-rig/data mine_ghsa.py; IMPL_SPEC §0 | unmined |

## Comic Narrator — publishable-quality push: expressive voice + music (CN, 2026-06-28)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CN-1 | H | **A "broken model" was a broken WAV write — torchaudio 2.12 clips int16 at 99.6%.** IndexTTS-2 inference was perfect; the output was noise. torchaudio 2.12 dropped the soundfile/sox backends and now routes `save()` through torchcodec, which reads the model's **int16** waveform tensor as float [-1,1] and saturates EVERY sample over 1.0 → 99.6% clipped (verified by reading raw samples: peak 1.0, RMS -0.0 dB, flat-factor 45). The fix is one shim — override `torchaudio.save` to write via `soundfile` (handles int16 natively). Lesson: when fresh audio output is garbage, inspect raw samples BEFORE blaming the model; the I/O layer lies. Any audio model saving via torchaudio ≥2.12 hits this. | comic-narrator IndexTTS-2 serving | unmined |
| CN-2 | M | **Expression = the ability to MOVE between registers, measurable as dynamic range.** Same line, same character ref: Fish Speech sat flat at -28 dB RMS regardless of emotion; IndexTTS-2 climbed -16.6 → -11.6 → -10.3 dB across neutral → determined → emotion-text. The "lifeless" complaint wasn't timbre — it was a single fixed register. A flat RMS across emotional takes is the signature of a TTS that reads instead of acts. | comic-narrator TTS A/B | unmined |
| CN-3 | M | **A limiter, not a normalizer — preserve the loudness RELATIONSHIP.** Per-line peak-normalizing would flatten a shout to the same level as a whisper, deleting the acting. Scale down ONLY when peak exceeds -1 dBFS; leave quieter lines alone. The dynamics between lines ARE the performance. | comic-narrator IndexTTS-2 save shim | unmined |
| CN-4 | M | **Short pages must score the PEAK beat, not the average.** A music-mood vote that needed ≥2 intense lines scored a shocked "WHAT? DUNGEON?!" page as calm (one shout drowned by neutral exposition). Comic pages are 1-4 lines; the underscore should track the most dramatic moment. Fix: peak-driven priority (one intense line → tense, ≥2 → action). Averaging is wrong when the sample is tiny and the signal is the outlier. | comic-narrator music.mood_from_tones | unmined |
| CN-5 | L | **Container mounts are not the host FS — a script ran "successfully" against a path that didn't exist.** ACE-Step runs inside creative-audio-worker (its venv reuses the container's SM_120 torch via --system-site-packages), but that container mounts only some /data/ai subdirs — not 08-portfolio or 06-configs. `docker exec … python /data/ai/08-portfolio/…/gen.py` failed with "No such file" despite exit 0 on the wrapper. Run from a mounted path, output to a mounted dir, host-copy the rest. | comic-narrator music-bed generation | unmined |

## Comic Narrator — v7 publish pass + adversarial QC (CN, 2026-06-29)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CN-6 | H | **The engines worked; the ASSEMBLY shipped the bugs.** A multi-agent QC of the v6 render said "not publishable" for two reasons that had nothing to do with the (genuinely good) voice + music engines: ~30% of runtime was bit-exact silence (every page opened on a 3s establishing-shot leader the music bed never covered — the muxer adelay'd the music-bearing narration *past* the overview), and the master clipped at +0.7 dBTP (hot TTS + tone gain summed past 0 dBFS in the pydub int16 mix). Both downstream of the models. Lesson: when judging a generative pipeline, audit the assembly/mux/mastering stage as hard as the models — that's where "the demo sounds great in isolation but the file is unshippable" lives. | comic-narrator v6→v7 QC | unmined |
| CN-7 | H | **Un-clipping looks exactly like flattening — and a careless reviewer would call the fix a regression.** v7 added 6 dB speech headroom + a normalize so nothing clips. Episode-wide RMS-std then "contracted" 7.07→4.13 dB — the textbook signature of squashed dynamics. It wasn't: v6's loud lines were CLIPPED FLAT (crest factor 9.5 dB), so they read as artificially consistent; v7 restored their crest to 12.0 dB and the shout-vs-neutral GAP grew (+8.53→+9.06 dB). The std dropped only because clipped peaks were pulled down into headroom. The metric that screams "regression" was measuring the removal of a defect. Always decompose an aggregate-variance change into its cause before calling it. | comic-narrator v7 voice-expression re-audit | unmined |
| CN-8 | M | **loudnorm can't hit in-band loudness AND sub--1 dBTP on peaky speech — you need a brickwall after it.** Targeting -15 LUFS landed -16.6 LUFS @ -1.34 dBTP (TP-safe but quiet); targeting -14 landed -15.3 LUFS @ -0.51 dBTP (in-band but TP over target). Raising loudness pushes peaks up; loudnorm undershoots whichever target it must to respect the other. Fix = loudnorm for loudness THEN an `alimiter` brickwall at -1.5 dBFS: loudness holds (~-15 LUFS), only the few over-ceiling transients are limited → -15.3 LUFS AND -1.25 dBTP, 0 clip. Two-pass loudnorm (measure→apply) also beats single-pass, which drifted to -0.20. | comic-narrator finalize_loudness | unmined |
| CN-9 | M | **My own onset-based sync check threw a false -500ms uniform offset; cross-correlation was the truth.** A regression skeptic's first A/V-sync method (audio-envelope onset vs SRT) reported a scary uniform -500ms drift; proper waveform cross-correlation (corr 0.97-1.00) showed -16..-43ms with no cumulative drift. The cheap measurement lied; the verdict held only because a second, stronger method was run before believing it. Adversarial verification has to include verifying your VERIFIER's method. | comic-narrator v7 av-sync re-audit | unmined |
| CN-10 | M | **The VLM mixes pixel and 0-1000-normalized bbox coords WITHIN one page — so sanitize per-box, not per-page.** Nemotron's char/sfx boxes are panel-local but loose: page_0004 panel-1's speaker came back `(460,250,490,720)` — overflows the 601×679 crop as pixels (x+w=950) but is valid 0-1000-normalized → `(276,170,294,489)`; meanwhile panel-2's boxes were honest pixels. No page-wide flag works. The old `_coerce_bbox` only handled 0-1 floats and missed this, pinning the speaker-spotlight halo to a panel edge. Fix = a per-box `_sanitize_bbox(b,pw,ph)`: keep if it fits; if it grossly overflows, adopt the ÷1000 reading *when that lands inside*; clamp a ≤12% overshoot (rounding) without shrinking; drop a box still mostly outside (no halo beats a wrong one). Apply at the producer so page.json is clean → both the halo and the area-ratio `derive_shot_type` get good data. | comic-narrator vision/nemotron_client `_sanitize_bbox`; tests/test_bbox_sanitize.py | unmined |

## Mushishi Bridge + the local-coding way out (MB, 2026-06-30)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MB-1 | H | **With a local agentic coder the bottleneck is the MODEL, not the harness.** A 3-way M0 build of the same spec on the same model proved it: vanilla CCR→Claude Code produced NOTHING (the vLLM `qwen3_coder` parser + the 30B drop the `<tool_call>` wrapper under load → tool calls leak as plain text and never execute — harness-independent, it hit Qwen Code too); Qwen Code completed but plausibly-broken (hallucinated registry, assumed probe.sh was JSON, broke auth); Opus was correct. Two separate ceilings — reliability (the parser leak) and quality (a 30B-A3B is junior). A nicer harness fixes neither; the real quality lever is VRAM. | mushishi-bridge M0 build-quality test | unmined |
| MB-2 | H | **Aider's diff format sidesteps the tool-call leak — so the "way out" is the loop + the oracle, not a Claude-Code clone.** Aider edits via SEARCH/REPLACE text blocks, not tool-calls, so the parser leak never applies. Wire it to a cheap oracle (`--auto-test` running pytest/lint/build): the local model can be wrong N times, the oracle catches it free, and Opus only reviews the GREEN diff. Proven twice incl. a real helper added to the Bridge's own codebase, zero Opus tokens in the loop. Gotcha: install Aider in its OWN venv — into system Python it clobbered hermes-agent's pinned deps. | qwenaider dogfood | unmined |
| MB-3 | M | **A build spec is only fair to a junior model if it's model-agnostic OR paired with a verification loop.** The Bridge spec was Opus-calibrated (read files, follow nuanced rules, self-verify); grading a 30B against it measured senior-ness it doesn't have. Either prescribe exact code, or attach an oracle that defines "done." | bridge SPEC vs local-model output | unmined |
| MB-4 | M | **Adversarial verification catches your own agents derailing — not just bad code.** In a fan-out workflow one implementation agent silently returned "output-style" meta-text and made zero edits; the integrate phase's independent check flagged `all_pass:false` and named the missing change, so I fixed it directly. Always add a verify/integrate phase, fan out by non-overlapping files, and know workflow subagents share the account session limit (a whole review round can die mid-flight and report 0 findings — which means 0 reviewers ran, not "clean"). | bridge D+B implement + review workflows | unmined |
| MB-5 | M | **To surface private data without spreading it: parser-in-repo, data-in-its-private-home, read live + auth-gated.** The Bridge's Creative tab lists the uncensored workflow catalogue, but the repo holds only the PARSER (`catalogue.py`); the private content stays in `~/Documents/business/` and is read at request time behind a bearer token, tailnet-only. The repo never carries the secret content. | bridge Creative tab | unmined |

## Ops fix batch + execution fan-out (OPS, 2026-07-02)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OPS-1 | H | **Cron GPU jobs must distinguish "VRAM held" from "GPU busy".** Map nvidia-smi PIDs → containers, act only on the gpu-tenants.sh canonical names, and reuse the `gpu_busy_report` idle verdict — an idle resident tenant is flushable+restorable (flush → run → `docker start` + :8000 verify + EXIT-trap restore), a foreign or working holder still means skip. workflow-maintenance.sh had skipped every cycle since 2026-07-01 behind an idle vllm-coding; idle-flush v2 cleared the overdue run same-day. | workflow-maintenance.sh idle-flush v2 | unmined |
| OPS-2 | H | **The backup "failure" was two bugs, neither the one reported:** (1) restic temp packs overflowed the tiny 804M /tmp partition on a full re-read (fix: `TMPDIR=/data/...` in backup.sh); (2) the LLM infra-digest was fed a snapshots-table FOOTER (no timestamps) plus its own previous digest text as "recent errors", so it hallucinated staleness dates and a fake repo path for days. Never let a summarizer see data without timestamps, and never feed a monitor its own output — the deterministic stamps were the only honest signals throughout. | backup.sh + infra-digest.sh fix 2026-07-02 | unmined |
| OPS-3 | M | **A fresh success sentinel is not success:** benchmark.sh stamped 2026-07 while measuring 0 tok/s (endpoint not serving) — join stamps WITH log outcomes. And the 8-alert morning of 07-01 collapsed to 3 root causes: alert on correlated causes, not per-job pings. | estate-brain.sh (THIS-8) | unmined |
| OPS-4 | M | **Encode a playbook as gate-terminated file scaffolds:** a CLI that structurally refuses to price, render, or ship converts "discipline in the operator's head" into structure without an agent framework; and verify stub commands against argparse, not their SPEC (the orchestrator had grown `--mode commercial` undocumented). | client-delivery thin slice (THIS-7) | unmined |
| OPS-5 | M | **Small local models need deterministic guardrails, not instructions:** qwen-coder-cpu ignored "do not duplicate these slugs" (100% dupes on attempt 1), merged 3 output formats into 1, and leaked an internal hostname as a hashtag — fix = per-section calls + code-side dedupe/URL-append/name-reject, retry at rising temperature. | content-coordinator.sh v2 (AI-4) | unmined |
| OPS-6 | M | **A compose file fixed in place is only half a fix — the doc block people copy from re-ships the bug.** The as-built forensic compose dropped `--moe-backend triton` + `VLLM_USE_FLASHINFER_MOE_FP4=1` in v1.5.1 (they BREAK NVFP4 MoE on SM120, vllm#34452), but the public spec doc kept prescribing both in FOUR places (compose block, flag table, troubleshooting, glossary) for six weeks. Same drift class as the five stop-list greps B2 killed: any duplicated operational truth needs a single source + references — docs that inline configs must be corrected in the same change as the config. Fixed in stack doc v1.8 (agent-mode + gpu-tenants.sh folded in, d5d5d9a). | sovereign-ai-stack doc v1.8 (BIZ-9 D2) | unmined |
| OPS-7 | L | **Owner decision 2026-07-02: phone number stays ON the public resume** (SG recruiters must be able to call) — reverses the same-day PII scrub for phone ONLY; address and all other PII stay off. Restored in src/content/resume.md + public/resume.md + regenerated PDF (372883b). | resume phone restore (372883b) | unmined |
| OPS-111 (renumbered from OPS-8 — collision with CRE-3 row; per OPS-50 ledger rule) | M | **Committed generated files must be regenerated with CI's exact toolchain, and a bot's "unresolved" flag can just be a wrong suggested diff.** qdrant-client PR #1247: (a) CI regenerates `local/async_qdrant_local.py` from the sync source and diffs — the first two commits only patched the sync client, a latent consistency-check failure nobody flagged; (b) regenerating on Python 3.12 produced spurious churn (`ast.unparse` tuple-paren drift vs CI's 3.10) and an unpinned ruff added more — clean output needed uv-fetched 3.10 + poetry.lock's ruff 0.4.3/autoflake 2.3.3; (c) CodeRabbit left its "major" batch finding unresolved only because its suggested fix used a nonexistent field (`request.search_params`; real field is `params`) so its match heuristic missed the pushed fix — verify bot findings against current code before "fixing" them. | qdrant-client PR #1247 review response (948d0ec) | unmined |
| OPS-8 | H | **I2V chain drift is a compounding failure, seams are not:** metric QA shows join deltas near zero (struct MAE 0.0027) while last-frame-vs-anchor color_L1 climbs 7.8→14.5 over 3 full segments — budget long clips by drift tolerance (ambient ≤3 seg, identity-critical ≤2), and use loop-mode (1/3 GPU cost, zero drift, motion reverses) for static shots. | CRE-3 chain-vs-loop QA (cre3_drift_qa) | unmined |
| OPS-9 | M | **Mode-switch teardown is a race:** agent-mode.sh slept 3s after stopping a ~29GB vLLM, but VRAM release lags → the next engine profiled into "KV cache -1.1GiB" and died. Fix = poll nvidia-smi for VRAM release (60s cap), never sleep a constant. | AI-5b shakedown; agent-mode.sh fix 2026-07-02 | unmined |
| OPS-10 | M | **Chat products: keep ALL policy in an engine + dumb transport adapters** — a mid-build Telegram→Discord swap cost ~nothing and a CLI adapter gave credential-free e2e the same night; and gate-by-construction beats gate-by-check (only owner-approved jobs can enter `queued`, so the GPU worker enforces approval with zero approval code). | creative production line (THIS-6) | unmined |
| OPS-11 | M | **Deterministic beats generative for status reporting:** the estate summary is fed to the digest LLM as an authoritative section AND appended verbatim — prompt discipline alone didn't survive a small model (it garbled "11 OK" into "Two OK" on the first wired send; the verbatim block carried the truth). Also: bash 5.2 `set -u` treats `${#arr[@]}` on a declared-but-never-assigned array as unbound — the all-green path was the untested one. | infra-digest estate wiring (FIX-6) | unmined |
| OPS-12 | M | **`build: .` containers drift silently from their repo** — rebuild+recreate after code changes; and without a .dockerignore, `COPY . .` bakes `.git` history and the live `.env` into a client-facing image. Doc defaults must also run cold: a README defaulting to an authed gateway with `LLM_API_KEY=not-needed` 401s for every stranger. | sovereign-legal-rag / rag-demo follow-ups | unmined |
| CN-11 | H | **A "residual list" is a snapshot, not a source of truth — re-verify against git before executing it.** The BIZ-9 list ordered a Track H install that git showed installed AND validated 06-13, then superseded by IndexTTS-2 the same day. Ten minutes of `git log` beat an evening of faithfully doing stale work. | comic-narrator BIZ-9 closeout | unmined |
| CN-12 | M | **"Need more voices" ≠ "need voice donors":** G8's male-voice gap was filled from 13 unused CC-BY-4.0 LibriTTS-R male speakers already on disk, curated by rerunning the bank's own median-F0 scan — pure CPU prep, one command from done. | comic-narrator G8 staging | unmined |
| OPS-13 | M | **Outward-facing counts drift fast:** proof-asset claims ("N PRs filed") were stale within 4 days of drafting — generate marketplace/résumé copy from one live-audited source (a gh query), never hand-copied numbers. | freelance docs housekeeping | unmined |
| OPS-14 | M | **A "stop everything" button gets clicked for "stop THIS thing" — offer the scoped stop.** Owner hit Mushishi Mode's StopAll to flush one vLLM and was surprised it swept the 8-container audio stack (by design, but no scoped alternative existed). Fix = new `stop-mode.sh` / "Stop Current Mode" row: stop only the primary GPU workload via the existing `stop_gpu_tenants_except <audio keep-set>` inverse — zero new stop logic. Bonus finds: audio-stop.sh printed "rq-dashboard preserved" while stopping it, and its restore hint predated coding-mode.sh — success/echo strings drift from the code they describe; test the *messages* too. | mushishi-mode v1.2.0 review | unmined |
| OPS-15 | S | **Outward copy is now generated, not written:** resume OSS section + marketplace PR counts regenerate daily from live gh status (opportunity-engine/oss-observer); edit oss-prs.json, never the text between the oss-auto markers. The first live audit caught drift both ways: the "11 PRs" claim included a closed-unmerged PR and the resume was missing 3 open ones. | oss-observer build | unmined |
| OPS-16 | S | **Publish the results, never the engine:** the sovereign-legal-rag showcase repo carries verbatim eval numbers, a live correct-refusal transcript, and a REDRAWN block-level diagram — model names, fusion mechanics, params, and prompts all stripped (grep-swept). Unverifiable claims omitted rather than repeated (a briefed number with no committed artifact stayed out). | sovereign-legal-rag-results build | unmined |
| OPS-17 | H | **Real-world validation arc (auditor Track A):** fixtures 1.00 → wild 0.00 (276 findings, 0 TP) → 5 root-caused rule bugs → v2 noise −90% (20→2, still 0 TP — real OSS doesn't leave these classes lying around) → seeded-CVE oracle test: TPs preserved 20/20, obvious FP rejected 20/20, subtle FP a 40% coin-flip CoT doesn't fix. Thesis partially supported: the LLM oracle preserves recall and filters obvious noise but can't judge value-provenance; asymmetric escalate-on-disagreement voting + taint-gate wiring are the next moves. Blog-worthy arc. | SovereignSec Track A v1→v2→oracle (2026-07-02) | queued |
| OPS-18 | M | **Five frontier LLMs, one shared blind spot:** planning consults with Gemini/ChatGPT/Grok/Kimi/DeepSeek on 3D-gen all missed the same load-bearing facts — TRELLIS.2 (the MIT open-weights leader, only Grok knew it), Tencent's Hunyuan3D license being void in EU/UK/KR, PolyGen/2.5/3.0 being closed, and neural retopo's non-viability. Chat-model consensus ≠ verified state of a fast field; three targeted research agents overturned the roster in one afternoon. Bonus market signal: raw AI generation is a $20/mo commodity while Kaedim charges ~$8/model for *cleaned QA'd* output — the margin is the cleanup/QA layer, so that's where the 3D-stack architecture puts its weight. | 3D-Stack v1.0 planning (2026-07-02) | unmined |
| OPS-19 | H | **"Restore what you flushed" is only half a protocol — the other half is "tear down what you brought up," in that order.** CPL's worker ported workflow-maintenance v2's idle-flush but put the ComfyUI teardown in an `elif` the flush path never reaches: it docker-started vllm-coding (needs 27.6GiB) into a card still holding its own ComfyUI (8.5GiB) → 355-restart OOM crash loop, 65 min, zero alerts. Three stacked gaps: (1) restore-before-teardown ordering; (2) `docker start` rc==0 read as "restored" — it only proves launch, a crash-loop looks like success; (3) nothing on the box watched RestartCount. Fixes: teardown-before-restore + persisted card-debt state (CPL + the reference impl's EXIT trap), restore now watches 45s and STOPS a looper instead of leaving it burning, new restart-loop-watch.sh (cron */10, delta≥3 or status=restarting → ntfy) + healthcheck backstop probe. Design lesson: an automation that starts GPU tenants must prove the tenant *stays up*, and any flush/restore pair must be symmetric (restore into the same card state you flushed from). | vllm-coding crash-loop incident RCA (2026-07-02) | unmined |
| OPS-20 | H | **The QA-gate layer earned its keep on day one — and headless Blender's exit-0-on-exception nearly hollowed it out.** P2 (td-meshops) fixture regression surfaced five real defects the gates caught honestly: silent-empty Blender results (Blender exits 0 when a `--python` script raises — `--python-exit-code 1` is mandatory, or your measurement stage returns {} and downstream reads face_count=0), a UV-overlap "0.9" that was the measuring rasterizer (bbox approx) not the unwrap, QuadWild's face count being patch-bound (identical 15,313 quads across every scaleFact/num recalibration → policy D16: quadriflow-with-target as budget default, QuadWild as waived quality mode), trimesh `percent=` decimation semantics giving 8 tris for a 1200 target (use `face_count=`), and a bake high/low mesh alias from an import helper that returns ALL scene meshes. Meta-lesson: when a gate fails, decide FIRST whether the assertion, the measurement, or the artifact is wrong — three of five "failures" were the measurement. End state: both fixtures S3→S10 green incl. OptiX bake, broken fixture repaired by S3, PrusaSlicer exit 0, glTF-Validator 0 errors, VRAM 2MiB idle throughout. | 3D-Stack P2 td-meshops build+acceptance (2026-07-03) | unmined |
| OPS-21 | H | **The golden-asset regression paid for itself the instant it ran: a real generated mesh is not a fixture.** P3 (3D orchestrator) fed P1's actual TRELLIS.2 1024³ output (962,944 faces) through P2's mesh chain and it OOM'd the whole box to 121GB RAM — twice. Fixtures were clean 252k-face Suzannes; a real generative mesh is ~1M faces of pathological micro-topology (thousands of tiny holes + disconnected voxel slivers), and P2's S3 `trimesh.split()` + pymeshlab `close_holes` are O(catastrophic) on it. The fix belonged in the S2→S3 *handoff* (orchestrator-owned), not P2's stage code: `fast_simplification` to ~80k first produces manifold-clean topology → the identical S3 then runs 0.3s at <1GB. Two more integration bugs the e2e caught: (a) the orchestrator judged pass/fail by reading `result["qa"]["verdict"]` while td-meshops returns a FLAT `{verdict,...}` → it "DELIVERED" an asset with three failing gates (silent-plausible-but-broken, the #1 AI-3D failure mode, reproduced in our own glue); (b) an idempotent library re-ingest overwrote FTS tags/caption with empty defaults and search went dark. Design lessons: benchmark on *real* outputs the moment upstream produces them (a fixture that's too clean hides the class of bug that matters); when crossing a service boundary pin the result SHAPE, not just the endpoint; a bounded retry policy that CONVERGES one gate (S3 non-manifold → voxel-remesh remedy → pass) and honestly PARKS another (S4 budget miss → needs-review with the QA report) is the system working, not failing; and a GEN VRAM guard should be headroom-aware (proceed on 30GB free without stop-the-world) so it never evicts a sibling session's idle context. | 3D-Stack P3 orchestrator + Potter e2e + library (2026-07-03) | unmined |
| OPS-22 | H | **A "recipe from an upstream example" hides three shapes of drift, and the same-object A/B is where the value shows.** P4 (Lane Q Hunyuan via ComfyUI) wired the kijai wrapper + MV-Adapter + StableDelight into the creative-comfyui container and ran the single-image-vs-multiview acceptance. Four install-time traps, each a different drift class: (1) **dependency version-triangle** — WanVideoWrapper pins `diffusers>=0.33` which pulls 0.38, but that needs a `transformers` symbol (`Dinov2WithRegistersConfig`) absent below 4.48 while transformers sat at 4.46 → BOTH new node packs import-failed; fix is bump transformers to 4.49 (keeps Wan's diffusers, adds the symbol), NOT downgrade diffusers (breaks Wan) — pin it AFTER the node-req installs so it wins on every boot; (2) **architecture-specific loader** — Hunyuan3D-2.1 needs its own `Hy3D_2_1SimpleMeshGen` node + nested-.ckpt (the generic loader assumes 2.0's config; a flattened-safetensors conversion is the wrong shape); (3) **symlinks in a bind-mount must be relative** — host-absolute targets dangle inside the container and ComfyUI silently skips them (empty model dropdown). Then the payoff: same mug, single-image Gilder **hallucinated 2× the depth** (bbox Y 1.65 vs 0.80) at 9.7GB/60s while 4-view 2mv constrained it at 6.2GB/18s — the numeric evidence the SOW's Track-A-premium/Track-B-standard tiering rests on. Also: the QA gates earned their keep on their first real artifacts — G1's global aspect check false-failed a legitimately-asymmetric object (mug handle → wide side, narrow front) until moved to opposing-pair-wise (same "measurement vs artifact" lesson as OPS-20); G14's render+SSIM+verdict all ran e2e but SSIM went negative because the reference had an opaque background vs the transparent mesh render (mask both before SSIM — deferred calibration). Bonus: the fal Multiple-Angles LoRA verified **Apache-2.0 → client-lane safe**, no quarantine. | 3D-Stack P4 Lane-Q + input hardening (2026-07-03) | unmined |

ALL six failed attempts were supply-chain: torch nightlies purged from the index within days (never pin nightlies in a Dockerfile), a frozen env.yml listing git-only versions (`utils3d==0.0.2`) and wheels the next python-minor dropped (`open3d==0.18.0` has no cp312), a PYTHONPATH module-shadow (`app.py` in the repo root vs a service package named `app`), and `|| echo` guards swallowing required sidecar steps. | OPS-46 | M | **Transfer-baking a texture from a generated thin-shell mesh onto its retopo is NOT a bake-settings problem — it's a WALL-AMBIGUITY problem, and no cage/ray/normals knob fixes it.** The retopo'd low surface sits BETWEEN the generated high's outer and inner shell walls (voxel-remesh drift ≈ shell thickness; measured 43% of surface points nearest-neighbour to the unpainted INNER wall), so Cycles selected-to-active rays and naive nearest-surface lookups alike sample the wrong wall — producing shredded patchwork that is INVARIANT to cage extrusion, max_ray_distance, winding, smooth/flat and explicit UV wiring (each eliminated empirically; a position-emission bake probe was the diagnostic that separated 'correspondence' from 'texture lookup'). The working fix is a deterministic normal-compatible nearest-vertex transfer (k-NN + normal-alignment + outward-side filter + EDT fill for genuinely unbakeable interior texels, ~19 s at 4K in scipy) — with a known residual: any nearest-surface method is noisy at relief-feature scale on a 20k-tri retopo, so textured HEROES either get a projection-cage bake or a ~100k LOD0 budget, while beauty/catalogue renders use the natively-textured Paint mesh directly. Glossary F59 (+F60: textured meshes score ~0.3 masked-SSIM vs ~0.9 untextured on G14 — threshold profiles must be texture-aware). | Gilder-Paint e2e + textured-hero session (2026-07-03) | unmined |

| OPS-48 | M | **A dimension gate failing "within ±2%" is usually not a scale bug — it's an axis-frame mismatch, and the fix is to make the deliverable match the CLIENT'S real dims, never to fake the target to the flawed mesh.** OPS-47's leftover: knit-sneaker/amazon-3d failed G12 at bbox 289×130×107 vs listed 300×110×120mm. The raw numbers looked "y/z swapped-ish", and they were — but the root cause was two compounded faults: (1) the batch maps the SKU's `L×W×H` string to `(x,y,z)` POSITIONALLY, assuming the mesh is x=L/y=W/z=H, but the single-view TRELLIS gen came out **Y-up (x=Length, y=Height, z=Width)**, so the listed W and H landed on the wrong mesh axes — a no-op on the earlier mug SKU (W=H=90) which is exactly why nobody caught it, and visible only once W≠H; (2) even axis-corrected, the Lane-C generation had drifted ~8% too tall / 3% short & narrow (the documented invent-the-unseen-axis weakness of single-image gen). The decision that matters: the listing is the client's ground truth and the marketplace shows it at real scale in AR, so the honest fix is to correct `target_dimensions_mm` into the mesh's own frame {x:300(L),y:120(H),z:110(W)} AND anisotropically rescale the shipped geometry (s6-uv/uv.glb — the real delivered base via `_apply_textures`' S6-UV fallback — plus the S5 LODs) to exactly that; setting the target to the flawed mesh's bbox would have "passed" the gate while shipping an AR asset that lies about the product's size (the silent-plausible-broken trap again). Orientation was proven three independent ways (sole footprint-taper, orthographic silhouettes, Blender's Y-up→Z-up importer) — don't guess a mesh's up-axis from numbers, measure it. UVs/albedo are scale-invariant so the F61 texture fix survived untouched; the rescaled shoe rendered as a correct undistorted runner. SYSTEMIC gap flagged: `_parse_dims`/S3 should map listed dims to the mesh's ACTUAL sorted axes, not positionally — the other showcase SKUs (headphones/lamp/backpack/chair, all W≠H) carry the same latent bug. Glossary F62; artifacts `/data/ai/07-cache/3d/ops47-sneaker-bbox/`. | knit-sneaker G12 dims fix (2026-07-03) | unmined |
| OPS-49 | M | **When one instance of a bug was fixed surgically, the systemic fix belongs at the data's point of truth, not at every consumer — and a coordinate-frame bug is a rank problem, not a position problem.** OPS-48 patched the knit-sneaker's dimensions by hand; this closed the flagged systemic follow-up so the four ungenerated showcase SKUs (headphones/lamp/backpack/chair, all W≠H) can't hit it. The positional `L×W×H → (x,y,z)` assumption lived in THREE places (S3 scale-discipline, host-side `spec_check` G12, delivery-time `gates.g12_amazon` G12), all assuming the mesh is x=Length/y=Width/z=Height — but the generator picks the up-axis freely (TRELLIS commonly Y-up). The clean fix is one small pure-python module (`dimframe.py`, no numpy/trimesh so it imports in every venv AND the meshops container) that maps each listed dim to the mesh axis of the same MAGNITUDE RANK (largest listed→largest extent) — orientation-invariant and deterministic. Two design calls worth keeping: (a) the bbox gate compares the sorted dimension SET, because "is it the right physical size" and "which way is up" are DIFFERENT questions and only the first is a scale gate's business — conflating them via positional compare was the entire bug; (b) scale stays uniform when the gen aspect already matches the listing (no gratuitous distortion) and only snaps anisotropically to the listed dims when the gen has drifted (the listing is AR ground truth — never fake the target to the mesh, per OPS-48). No manifest mutation = minimal blast radius. Proven three ways: new `test_dim_frame.py` 20/20 (incl. a W≠H Y-up mesh), `test_p5.py` 36/36 no-regression, and a re-run of G12 on all 6 delivered profiles where the knit-sneaker flips OLD-positional-FAIL→NEW-PASS from the RAW CSV dims (the systemic fix subsumes the surgical one) while mug/gizmo stay green; `gates.g12_amazon` + `s3_clean` re-verified live inside `td-meshops`. CPU-only, no GPU, no re-gen. Glossary F63; `.bak-f63-20260703` on 3 files. | 3D systemic dimension-frame fix (2026-07-03) | unmined |

| OPS-70 | M | **An NVIDIA driver installed from the `.run` installer WITHOUT the `dkms` package is a time-bomb — the next kernel update orphans the module and the GPU silently vanishes on reboot.** 2026-07-05 root-cause of F74: driver 595.80 was installed via NVIDIA's `.run`, which left a DKMS-ready tree at `/usr/src/nvidia-595.80` (`AUTOINSTALL=yes`) but the `dkms` PACKAGE was never installed — so when unattended-upgrades pulled kernel 6.8.0-134 and the reboot switched onto it, nothing rebuilt the module. `.ko` files existed only under `/lib/modules/6.8.0-124` → `nvidia-smi` dead, `/dev/nvidia*` gone, `modinfo nvidia` not found, `lsmod` empty. This session is non-interactive (no sudo), so the fix was STAGED not run: idempotent `restore-nvidia-driver.sh` at `/data/ai/01-workspace/scripts/` — installs `dkms`, `dkms add`+`install` for the running kernel (595.80 was verified to test-compile cleanly against -134 before writing the script), loads the compute modules, creates device nodes, and pins `/etc/modules-load.d/nvidia.conf` so future boots auto-load (nothing did before). Secure Boot is DISABLED → no MOK signing. Two adversarial-review hardenings baked in: (1) SKIP `nvidia_drm` when a display manager is live — loading the KMS module can make Xorg seize GPU-0 and reset the desktop, and `nvidia_drm` is NOT needed for CUDA/`nvidia-smi`; (2) on a modprobe-after-build failure, dump `dmesg` and point at the boot-into-6.8.0-124 fallback. The historical "small `/tmp`" problem (804M part, 737M free) only bites the `.run` path (self-extract+build) — DKMS builds under `/var/lib/dkms` on `/` (138G) and never touches `/tmp`; the `.run` fallbacks therefore pass `--tmpdir=/home/mushi/nvidia-temp`. Rule: any driver installed via `.run` MUST use `--dkms` AND have the `dkms` package present (else register the tree by hand), or the very next kernel bump kills the GPU; and a "black GPU" triage starts at the driver layer (`lsmod\|grep nvidia`, `ls /dev/nvidia*`, `dkms status`) BEFORE blaming any stack. | NVIDIA driver DKMS root-cause + restore script (2026-07-05) | unmined |

The 3D GPU IMAGES are all built (`mushishi/td-trellis2:p5`, `mushishi-meshops:latest`, `td-unirig:p6`, `td-omni:p6`) — `3d-ctl status` mislabels them "not built" because it greps a different tag. Fix STAGED (needs sudo): idempotent `restore-nvidia-container-toolkit.sh` at `/data/ai/01-workspace/scripts/` — repo already configured, `apt-get install` the four packages, **`apt-mark manual` them so autoremove can't sweep them again (the recurrence fix)**, `nvidia-ctk runtime configure`, restart docker (brings back the ~22 post-reboot casualties incl. the crawl4ai MCP), then a host-nvidia-smi-injection smoke test. Rules: (1) `default-runtime=nvidia` makes the toolkit load-bearing for ALL containers — treat its removal as a full-container-plane outage, not a GPU-only one; (2) "containers won't start" triage = `docker inspect -f '{{.HostConfig.Runtime}}'` + exit-127 → check `which nvidia-container-runtime` before the stack; (3) any nvidia-driver purge/clean-up must reinstall the toolkit after. | NVIDIA container-toolkit purge RCA (2026-07-05) | unmined |

| PLAT-P6 | M | **Conductor P6 (evals + prompt registry + guardrails, PR #21): three reusable platform patterns.** (1) **A CI eval gate must be deterministic + network-free** — golden-set scoring runs against canned FIXTURES (the expected-good answers as a JSON baseline), not a live model, so it runs on a GitHub-hosted runner with no GPU/self-hosted runner (T11) and a regression shows up as a reviewable fixtures diff. The live-model run is a separate on-box job. (2) **A "canary" makes the gate provably bite**: a golden case whose fixture is deliberately BAD, asserted to FAIL — if it ever passes, the criteria went toothless and the gate blocks; that is what turns "the gate blocks bad prompts" from a claim into a test (proven: a simulated regressing PR → `eval-gate` exit 1, restored → exit 0). (3) **Off-by-default safety features need TWO policy gates AND-ed** — a deployment flag (env, default false) plus a per-job opt-in — and the OFF path must be a hard no-op (the code returns None and the hot path never touches guardrail code), so "off by default" is structural, not a branch you can forget. Also: **stdlib-only when the dep budget is frozen** — the eval framework + prompts-as-code export use JSON, not PyYAML/deepeval, matching the registry's existing hand-rolled YAML parse; a new runtime dep on a public CI runner is a supply-chain cost, avoid it for a self-contained feature. Gotcha logged: Fastated route order matters — `/{name}/aliases` must be declared before `/{name}/{version}` or `aliases` parses as the int version; and unbounded int path/body params overflow SQLite (int64) → a 500 under schemathesis, so bound them to `2**63-1` for a clean 422. | Conductor P6 (2026-07-07) | unmined |

Bugs: (F02) AGE's `create_graph('nui')` makes a schema matching the login role, so unqualified `CREATE TABLE` in 02-relational.sql landed in `nui` not `public` — fixed by pinning `SET search_path=public` (and had to `compose build` the db since init SQL bakes at build time). (F01) the DP-P0-1 AGE-on-PG18 risk resolved clean (`AGE_REF=master` → 1.8.0, no pg17 fallback). | ISAZA-P0 | M | **ISAZA P0 (Mizo↔English translation stack foundations) built by reusing the proven Tanyuu/GINKO P0 scaffold, with a new load-bearing piece: a sample-level provenance ledger + a license-CI that fails-closed on non-commercial data crossing into a commercial training set.** `isaza-gateway` (:9250, FastAPI stub — live /health+/metrics, 8 frozen v1 endpoints 501-until-P2) + `isaza-db` (:9255, PG16+pgvector — no AGE, unlike Tanyuu/NUI: Isaza's store is relational+vector only). Reused: `create_app()`+isolated-CollectorRegistry gateway, digest-pinned uv-baked Dockerfiles, 01/02 db-init split, the ruff/mypy/pytest/gitleaks/pip-audit CI, the ledger-restore-verify pattern. **New pattern (the real deliverable): D9 license hygiene enforced at THREE layers** — (1) a pydantic `ProvenanceRow` validator makes an NC/quarantined/unresolved row claiming Track-B *unwritable*; (2) a `license_ci.check_manifest` gate rejects a manifest that references such a source (the CI + pre-commit gate); (3) a Postgres `CHECK` constraint blocks it at the relational layer. Proven by a **planted red-team fixture** (a Track-B ASR manifest pulling in NC Bible audio) that the CI rejects with exit 1 — the D9 "NLLB-was-mislabeled-MIT" failure-mode made mechanically impossible. **Contract-first**: the frozen OpenAPI (`contracts/isaza-api-v1.yaml`) is *rendered from* the pydantic contract and a CI check fails on any drift, so a breaking API change can't land silently. **Research-pass lessons (D13 honesty in the data layer):** always verify a corpus's ACTUAL license by fetching the page, don't trust the spec's assumption — the spec's "ENLUS CC-BY-4.0" was UNVERIFIABLE (no public repo locatable, ACM/ResearchGate 403-walled) so it's tagged `unknown` and license-CI-blocked until an author email confirms it; the open "does FineTranslations have Mizo?" question resolved YES (90,411 lus docs, ODC-BY, but machine-translated → a *quality* gate is still needed, separate from the license gate); FLORES+ is GATED (needs HF token) + ShareAlike (eval-only, never in a redistributed mix). Lessons: (1) fail-closed is the only safe default for a license gate — unknown/academic licenses count as NOT-clean, never assumed-permissive; (2) enforce a critical invariant at every layer it can be violated (model, CI, DB), not just one; (3) render the API contract from code + diff it in CI so "frozen" actually means frozen; (4) an enum that lacks a real-world value (ODC-BY, Gemma-Terms) should be noted as a tracked additive-bump, not silently mislabeled — document the true license in the human-readable matrix meanwhile. | ISAZA P0 foundation build (2026-07-07) | unmined |
| ISAZA-P3PREP | H | **ISAZA P3-PREP (the PWA client shell): build the whole client against a FROZEN contract while the models are still 501, by generating the UI's types from the same pydantic contract that backs the server + running everything on a labelled mock.** The load-bearing pattern is **contract-as-single-source-of-truth across the Python/TS boundary**: a python script dumps the pydantic contract models to a JSON Schema (committed), `json-schema-to-typescript` renders `types.gen.ts` (committed), and a CI drift-check regenerates both and FAILS on any divergence — so a server-side contract change can't silently rot the client, and the client's `{confidence, model_track, model_version, alternatives[]}` types are provably the ones the gateway serves. (Subtlety: the frozen *OpenAPI YAML* only fixed request bodies with empty response schemas; the RICH envelopes live in the pydantic module — generate from the pydantic models, not the thin YAML.) The WS streaming frame shapes didn't exist in the contract, so they were added **additively** (new pydantic models, OpenAPI YAML byte-unchanged, all existing tests green) — a new message kind is additive, a changed field is a MAJOR event. **Mock discipline (so a mock can NEVER ship as real):** the mock is a Vite dev plugin (`apply:"serve"` → excluded from `vite build`) + a standalone loopback server; every response carries `x-isaza-mock:1` + a `mock-*` version + the UI paints a permanent "MOCK MODE" banner + the "translation" is an obviously-synthetic gloss, never plausible target text. **The axe a11y gate earned its keep**: it caught a REAL WCAG-AA contrast failure (a placeholder at `opacity:0.7` → 3.62:1 on white, needs 4.5:1) that a screenshot review would have waved through — a11y-as-a-required-CI-gate, not polish. Also: **offline is proven against the PRODUCTION service worker, not the dev server** — vite-plugin-pwa's dev SW doesn't precache unbundled modules, so the offline-reload test runs against a `vite build && vite preview` project (the phrase bank is a static `import`, so it's in the bundle and survives offline regardless of SW). Estate honesty: the preview harness's JPEG-screenshot capture reliably timed out while eval/click/snapshot/resize all worked — used Playwright's own screenshots for the PR proof (the app was fully verifiable via the accessibility snapshot + driven flows). Lessons: (1) generate cross-language types from the ONE source + gate drift in CI, or "frozen contract" is a lie the moment two languages diverge; (2) a mock must be structurally un-shippable (dev-only plugin) AND loudly self-identifying (header + banner + fake-looking output), not just "we'll remember to swap it"; (3) make a11y a hard CI gate — axe finds contrast/role bugs a human screenshot pass rubber-stamps; (4) test offline against the real build's SW, not the dev server; (5) when the model isn't ready, you can still ship + fully test every UI seam (confidence UX, corrections→feedback, streaming+degrade, read-back trigger) against the contract + a mock — the shell doesn't wait on the model. | ISAZA P3-PREP PWA client shell (2026-07-08) | unmined |
