| SEC-P6 | H | **NUI P6 (AI/LLM & agentic-system security engine, static/config half) — the OWASP LLM Top-10 become DETECTORS over an agent app AT REST, and the whole thing runs with no key, no network, no model.** `nui-ai` (:9308) audits the client's OWN AI systems (system prompt + tool defs + MCP manifests + RAG corpus), not their code — likely NUI's LEAD go-to-market wedge. Three reusable lessons: (1) **a "new engine" over a frozen graph is a MAPPING exercise, not a schema one** — an AI finding is still a `VULN_FINDING`, an agent tool is still a callable (`FUNCTION`); project the OWASP-LLM id onto the closest CWE so it rides the ONE `matches_cwe` correlation spine with code findings (an AI over-permissioned tool and a code SSRF compose into one attack-path), and put the OWASP-LLM id in the node NAME — never invent `AI_FINDING` (that splinters the graph the product is built on). (2) **prompt-injection detection is a TAINT-style reachability check, not a jailbreak-string scan** — model the agent's dataflow (untrusted sources: user msg / tool output / RAG chunk / MCP resource → the LLM → real-world sinks: shell/http/email/sql) and flag `untrusted → LLM → sink` with no interposed guardrail/human-gate; report the concrete path, proof-carrying, exactly like a code source→sink. That framing (structural, not string-matching) is why a code-audit shop is uniquely credible at AI-system audit, and it emits NO attack payloads (defensive, never offensive). (3) **the clean-control ZERO-findings gate is really a secret-scanner PRECISION test** — a naive entropy-only scan false-positives on `your_api_key_here` (entropy 3.29 > 3.0); needs a placeholder FP-guard (`your_`/`example`/`changeme`/`<`/…) AND matched-value REDACTION before surfacing (you must be able to prove you never persisted the secret you found in a client's prompt/corpus). The static acceptance (over-permissioned tool + injection-reachable sink + system-prompt secret leak flagged; ZERO on the clean control) needs no owner key; the model-assisted SEMANTIC leg uses the P1 fake for plumbing and gate-skips the live model pending the LiteLLM key. **P6 COMPLETES NUI v1 (P0–P6).** | NUI P6 AI/LLM security build (2026-07-08) | unmined |
| SEC-P7 | H | **NUI P7 (delivery backbone + cryptographic audit attestation) — how you turn "a PDF" into a court-admissible, independently-verifiable audit record, and why "verify offline with just a public key" beats installing a signer.** `nui-report` (:9307) renders a finding set to SARIF/PDF/CI + binds the WHOLE engagement into a signed, hash-chained SLSA-style attestation. Four transferable lessons: (1) **when a spec names a signing TOOL, implement the PROPERTY with what's pinnable** — the spec floated minisign/age but the box has only gpg (a stateful keyring dep the client's auditor would also need); signing with **ed25519 via the `cryptography` lib** means verification needs nothing but the embedded public key + the pinned lib — no keyring, no agent, no network, no system package. That's a *stronger*, more portable "verify offline" guarantee than any external signer, and it's deterministic so the one-byte-tamper test is trivial. Don't take sudo to install what the language already gives you. (2) **content-addressing a bitemporal graph means hashing the VALID-TIME facts and EXCLUDING the transaction-time metadata** — the attestation binds "the exact graph snapshot," so the snapshot hash must be reproducible; include the per-ingest `recorded_at` wall-clock and the same graph hashes differently every run, breaking the whole property. Hash what was true about the world (kind/name/proof-status/confidence/evidence-hash), drop when-we-recorded-it; the determinism test catches it. (3) **the liability boundary is part of the SIGNED record** — scope (assessed vs NOT-assessed) + limitation-of-liability-capped-at-fee + advise-not-certify (D10) are bound into the attestation subject AND stamped in every artifact footer, so they can't be silently edited after signing; an attestation that only signs the findings but not the terms is a liability landmine. (4) **fpdf2 gotchas for a portable renderer** (weasyprint needs cairo/pango, absent): built-in Helvetica is latin-1-only (map `—`/`→`/curly-quotes), `multi_cell(w=0)` loops/errors on a long unbreakable `sha256:…` token after cursor drift (reset X + pass explicit width + CHAR-wrap), and a `footer()` that wraps with auto-page-break ON recurses infinitely (disable it while drawing the footer). Acceptance (valid schema-checked SARIF + client PDF w/ method+caveats + attestation verifies AND catches a one-byte tamper + scoped profile + review UI behind tailscale-serve HTTPS) proven fully offline, no owner key. | NUI P7 delivery backbone build (2026-07-08) | unmined |
| OPS-113 | M | **ADASHINO P1 (solver + safety kernel, Opus fork 2026-07-08): three transferable lessons from property-testing a safety-critical deterministic core.** (1) **Property tests find ROUNDING bugs code review misses:** the ≤10% week-over-week load-cap constraint computed `prev×1.10` then `round(_,1)` — for prev=10.5 that's 11.55 → rounds UP to 11.6, 0.05 OVER the safety cap. Hypothesis property (c) found it in minutes at 400 examples; fix = floor-to-0.1kg in `shrink_load` so a capped value can never round back above its cap. Any "clamp then round" in a safety invariant is suspect — round TOWARD the safe side. (2) **Make only-shrink a CHECKED contract, not a convention:** the kernel safety pass orders plan lines by a scalar `safety_size` and structurally verifies (a) every output line traces to a solver input line and (b) total size is non-increasing per constraint — a constraint that grows/adds raises `SafetyEnforcementError` and aborts. §14.1-2h (`safety ⊆ solver`) became a by-construction guarantee instead of a hoped-for property. (3) **Golden snapshots must be OFFLINE-reproducible or they'll flake:** first cut generated 12-week golden plans from the live DB — which mixes a flaky external API (wger) into the drift gate AND risks normalize-vs-enrich path divergence. Fix = build the golden ontology purely from committed sources (public-domain free-exercise-db slim fixture + owner seed + rules JSON); byte-identical across processes, CI needs no DB/network. Honest profile note: 10k Hypothesis examples × 8 invariants = ~20 min — pin TWO profiles (`acceptance` 10k for push/nightly, `ci` 400 for the PR gate) and document both in the test file, don't quietly ship the small one. Also: an allocation bug ("16×8 chest press" — a muscle group's whole weekly volume piled onto one lift) shows solvers need domain-shaped output caps (≤4 sets/exercise, split across lifts), not just constraint-satisfaction. PR: MushiSenpai/adashino#2. | ADASHINO P1 build (2026-07-08) | unmined |
| OPS-114 | H | **Monthly workflow-drift rebuild (2026-07-08) + same-day owner follow-up: a drift *detector* that only knows "missing node type" conflates failure classes needing opposite responses — and the automated run itself MISDIAGNOSED one of them, which the follow-up corrected.** The validator flagged 5 workflows. Class 1 — REAL drift (renamed node, rewire): none this run. Class 2, first read as "uninstalled node pack belonging to the 3D stack's own ComfyUI" — **WRONG, corrected same day**: the 3× `3d-*` graphs run on creative-comfyui BY DESIGN (Lane-Q `install_into_comfyui.sh`), and the real cause was a **three-layer ephemeral-state regression**: (a) F14b's "durable" fix lived in `start.sh`, but start.sh is `COPY`'d into the image at BUILD time — editing it does nothing until `docker compose build`, so the fix only existed as live pip state in the then-running container; (b) the 07-07 container recreation (driver-recovery day) booted the stale June-15 image → trimesh gone → ALL Hy3D*/MVAdapter nodes vanished, compiled custom_rasterizer gone too; (c) recreation used the base compose alone → the P4 override's RW mounts silently dropped. The Dockerfile itself already documented this exact class (06-13: SAM3/SeedVR2 deps "kept vanishing on container recreation → BAKED to kill pip-rot") — the known pattern just wasn't applied to the next layer of deps. Fix: bake everything (deps + essentials + prebuilt sm_120 rasterizer wheel) into the image, merge the override mounts into the base compose, delete the now-poisonous stale `transformers==4.49.0` boot-pin (would downgrade the working 5.9 state), then PROVE it by recreating the container and smoke-rendering a 903k-vert Hy3D mesh from the fresh image. Class 3 — retired-by-design graphs re-flagged monthly because the retirement was prose in COVERAGE.md, never encoded in the checker's skip-list (2-line fix). Meta-lessons: (1) classify missing-node as rename vs uninstalled vs retired vs REGRESSED — the checker can't, so the human/agent loop must; (2) anything installed into a RUNNING container is a time bomb with a fuse labeled "next recreation" — bake into the image or mount it; (3) a boot-time pip pin ages into a downgrade attack on your own stack once the resolver state moves on; (4) "we decided X" in prose ≠ enforcement — encode it in the tool; (5) the automated run's honest "couldn't rebuild, here's why" was still the right output — but its confident misdiagnosis shows detection agents should say "nodes missing, cause unknown" unless they've checked container logs, not guess topology. | this run + owner follow-up; COVERAGE.md 2026-07-08 §Monthly drift check (corrected); creative-stack Dockerfile bake 2026-07-08; 3D P&S F-row 2026-07-08 | unmined |
| OPS-115 | M | **/data extended onto a second 2TB SSD with mergerfs — zero-downtime shadow-mount pattern + two portable gotchas.** /data (single ext4, 94% full, 20+ containers + llama-server holding it open) grew to a 3.3T pool WITHOUT stopping anything: (1) chose mergerfs over LVM-span deliberately — LVM linear across 2 disks means EITHER disk dying kills the whole fs (your 1.4TB of models hostage to the newest drive), needs a full backup/wipe/restore, and there was no 1.4TB of staging space; mergerfs keeps each disk an independent ext4 (independent failure domains), keeps every hardcoded /data/ai/... path identical, and FUSE overhead is irrelevant for big sequential model reads. (2) the **zero-outage trick**: mount the old ext4 at a SECOND mountpoint (/mnt/data-disk1 — same superblock, kernel allows it), then mergerfs shadow-mounts OVER the still-mounted /data with `-o nonempty`; every pre-existing fd/bind-mount keeps working against the underlying mount, new opens go through the pool, and reboot lands in the clean fstab-only state (branches + `fuse.mergerfs` line with `x-systemd.requires-mounts-for=` on both branches — the nonempty flag is only for the live shadow, not fstab). (3) gotchas: **lsblk NAME output embeds tree glyphs (├─/└─)** — scripting `lsblk -pno NAME | sed -n 2p` hands you `└─/dev/nvme0n1p1` and every `[ -b ]` test silently fails; always use `-l` list mode when parsing. And **installing a disk renumbers /dev/nvme*** (old nvme0→nvme1, system nvme1→nvme2) — the UUID-based fstab shrugged it off, but any script/doc naming bare nvme devices is wrong after a hardware change; the setup script targets /dev/disk/by-id/<model_serial>. Placement: `category.create=mfs` + `moveonenospc=true` + `minfreespace=10G` → new writes land on the emptier (new) disk, old data stays put, no rebalancing needed or wanted. Script: /data/ai/01-workspace/scripts/data-pool-setup.sh (idempotent, re-runnable). | /data pool expansion (2026-07-09) | unmined |

| OPS-117 | H | **The "Unreal-ready" previs/hero claim shipped UNTESTED in-engine — and the first real audit (this session) found every prop imports 10× too large (F93).** The pack was gate-proven (valid glTF, manifold, Nanite flag set) but nobody had ever run its own import script inside Unreal; the promise "drops into your scene on the first try" rode on "it produces valid files," which is necessary-not-sufficient. Root: meshes are authored at the mm spec but the FBX carries native-cm `UnitScaleFactor=1.0`, so UE reads mm-as-cm (a 1.8 m crowd figure → 18 m). Orientation is actually fine on the FBX path (export bakes the Y→Z stand-up; the OPS-111 +Y-lying trap only bites a GLB fallback). Fixed by adding `import_uniform_scale = 0.1` (mm→cm) to the generator template + both shipped import scripts + both site copies, regression-tested (test_previs 3/3). market_stall is a separate open outlier (F94: ~4.4× + wrong aspect vs its own spec — logged, not fixed). Outward claims corrected HONESTLY (not over-claimed): the previs case-study page + 3D-CAPABILITY-MATRIX now say "Unreal-import-correct, in-engine render pending" — NOT "tested in UE 5.8", because UE5 is not installed (browser-gated download; 403 anon / 404 to this gh account). The "shot in the engine-agnostic viewport" demo clip was clarified as an offline Blender blocking preview, not an Unreal capture. Full in-engine proof (viewport screenshots + live Nanite stat) is a ONE-COMMAND staged harness at `/data/ai/01-workspace/unreal/validation/run-validation.sh` that runs the moment the owner lands the UE 5.8 zip. MCP channel evaluated license-gate-first: winner `ChiR24/Unreal_mcp` (MIT); the two most-named candidates (kvick-games, chongdashu) FAIL the gate (no LICENSE → NOASSERTION, F52 discipline) despite chongdashu's 2026★. Lessons: (1) an "engine-ready" deliverable must be imported into the engine at least once — "valid files" is not "usable in a scene"; (2) a workaround found in an ADJACENT tool (the Blender demo's own scale/axis fix) is a red flag the real deliverable's contract has the same unhandled trap; (3) correct the claim to match reality, not to the aspiration — "import-correct, render pending" is the honest state today. **CORRECTION + COMPLETION (2026-07-10, same session, UE 5.8.0 INSTALLED + validation RUN IN-ENGINE): the engine invalidated two of this row's claims — the scale error is 1000×, not 10× (UE reads the FBX as METERS; the byte-level parse misread UnitScaleFactor; correct factor = 0.001, F96), and orientation is NOT fine on the FBX path (crowd_figure imports lying along +Y — F97; fixed via manifest-driven per-asset import_rotation). Validation reached overall_green=True on attempt 6-class runs: every spec'd prop at true size (cannon 1.7×4.2×1.4 m, lamp 3.22 m, crowd 1.8 m standing), Nanite ON both heroes, real viewport renders (SceneCapture2D — HighResShot writes black under -RenderOffscreen, F98). Guard lessons en route: wall-clock timeout killed a healthy 90-min first-compile (F95); ExecCmds inner quotes silently run `py` with no arg (idle editor forever); log-silence isn't hang (use process CPU accrual); export_render_target defers — capture and export need a tick between them. The engine is the only ground truth for engine claims — a file-level fix published before an engine run can itself be the bug.** | UNREAL-VALIDATION session (2026-07-09): F93/F94, import-script fix, case-study + matrix honesty correction, staged UE5 harness + MCP verdict | unmined |
| OPS-118 | L | **A container can run correct-and-"unhealthy" forever when the healthcheck comes from the IMAGE, not your compose file — monitoring-ntfy-bridge sat at a 265-probe failing streak while publishing alerts fine.** xenrox/ntfy-alertmanager bakes in `curl localhost:8080/health`, but our config.scfg binds :8111, so every probe since deploy failed with connection-refused; the compose service defined NO healthcheck, so nothing in our repo showed why. Fix = explicit compose healthcheck override probing :8111 (chosen over changing the listen port, which would ripple into Alertmanager's webhook URL). Lesson: any image whose port you reconfigure needs its baked-in HEALTHCHECK audited/overridden in compose — an inherited check silently tests the DEFAULT config, not yours. (2026-07-10) |
| OPS-119 | M | **A single stray double-quote in the session-authored `PROJECT-STATUS.yaml` 500-ed KARIBUSA's `/api/status`, and the dashboard's offline SAMPLE fallback then invented false red alerts (\"VictoriaMetrics DOWN since 07-04\", disks at 90%) — none real.** URO's `status_line` was a double-quoted YAML scalar containing an unescaped `\"GPU idle → load nemotron\"`, so PyYAML aborted at line 431 col 1610; `build_status()` did a bare `yaml.safe_load` so the parse error propagated to a 500, and the front-end swapped in canned sample telemetry that reads like a live outage. Two-part fix: (1) escaped the interior quotes (real telemetry: 28up/11down/0unhealthy, /data 58%, VM actually HTTP-200 up); (2) hardened the loader — a `yaml.YAMLError` now degrades to estate-level status (docker/gpu/disk don't need the YAML) with an accurate alert naming the bad line, instead of blinding the whole board. Lesson: when a canonical file is hand-edited by EVERY session, the reader must treat it as untrusted input — degrade with a precise error, never fail-open into sample data that fabricates alarms. Also: a dashboard's \"offline sample\" mode must be unmistakably labelled so a stale render is never mistaken for live red. (2026-07-11) |
| VISHWAKARMA-P0 | M | **VISHWAKARMA P0 (Foundations & sim, Opus fork 2026-07-09) — three transferable build lessons.** (1) **A safety clamp can be CORRECT but not IDEMPOTENT, and that's a hardware bug.** The clamp-then-round-toward-safe kernel (ADASHINO OPS-113) passed "never exceeds a bound," but a joint value already on the 1e-3 grid (`0.021`) serializes to JSON and reads back as `0.020999999999999908`, so `floor((x-lo)/res)` dropped a WHOLE quantum on replay — flagging every recorded frame as "not envelope-safe" and, on hardware, silently shifting every commanded position down. Property tests caught it because they assert idempotency across a serialization round-trip, not just first-pass correctness. Fix: an epsilon grid-snap (`floor(raw + 1e-9·max(1,|raw|))`) + a denormal-resolution guard (hypothesis found a NaN-grid edge at res=1e-38). Rule: safety/quantization code must be tested for round-trip idempotency, and a hypothesis strategy must be constrained to REALISTIC inputs (a denormal servo resolution isn't a real command grid — guard it in code AND narrow the strategy). (2) **Pin a dataset FORMAT to the pinned library's TAG, not its head docs.** LeRobot's on-disk layout changed between v2.1 (one parquet/episode + `meta/episodes.jsonl`) and v3.0 on `main` (sharded parquet + `meta/episodes/`); the head docs describe v3.0 but the pinned v0.6.x reads v2.1. A writer coded from the head docs produces a dataset the pinned reader can't load. Verified the writer against the v0.6.0-TAGGED `datasets/utils.py`, stamped `codebase_version="v2.1"`. (3) **Make the sim/heavy stack an on-box extra so the CI gate stays offline+GPU-free.** The round-trip acceptance runs a pure-python LeRobot writer/reader (no torch/mani_skill/lerobot); the real ManiSkill3 headless render + `lerobot`-loads-it are separate on-box steps. This mirrors the estate "CI is offline, on-box is heavy" split (ISAZA/Tanyuu) and let the whole P0 gate — 43 tests incl. the license-CI red-team fixture rejection — run without ever touching the GPU (which held a live Unreal tenant that must not be stopped, D10). Repo MushiSenpai/vishwakarma (private); PR on phase/p0-foundations. | VISHWAKARMA P0 build (2026-07-09) | unmined |

G16/F109/silhouette all PASSED: no gate checks WHICH texture got bound, only that one did. Caught it only because the rework protocol re-measures the SHIPPED artifact's contrast after every export (the addendum-3 lesson, now proven twice: check the shipped bytes, not the stage output). Fixes with legs: (1) any `glob(...)[0]` over a dir that also holds `.orig-*`/`.bak-*` siblings is a latent wrong-file bind — exclude backups AND sort deterministically; (2) a backup convention (`albedo.orig-prerefmatch.png`) that matches the same glob key as the live file is a trap by construction — backups should not share the discovery key. Second lesson from the same session: an honest metric needs a defect-specific threshold — at ΔE>16 the "speckle" count was ~flat pre/post fix because legitimate carbon-weave grain dominates; at ΔE>40 (the actual bright-chip class) the fix showed −16% clusters/−27% texels. A metric that mixes signal classes will report your real improvement as noise. And the judge remains its own hazard: the SAME unchanged anchor frames scored 4.5 then 7.0 a day apart (n=3 votes each) — vote counts tame variance per-run, not across runs; calibration bands need re-baselining before any drift conclusion. | F110 texture-lane rework (2026-07-12) | unmined |

Then the G17 judge (n=5, nemotron) failed it identically to the previous iteration — matches_reference [2,0,0,0], "damaged" ×4 — and my own render read agreed: the crate's dominant defects are the ALBEDO projection (wet-plastic smear, carbon-weave→speckle, a hard mirror-seam discontinuity down the centreline), and the seam grooves, however perfect, are a second-order feature the smear buries. **Lessons: (1) a named sub-fix can be 100%-complete-and-verified and still not move the judge — the anchor moving is the real success metric, not the sub-task's own numbers; measure anchor movement, not just "did I build what was asked." (2) A crate that reads "damaged" to the judge with L1 confirming ZERO geometry holes and a clean silhouette is a TEXTURE failure wearing a geometry costume — the defect class matters more than the defect location. (3) Honest PARK on a passing sub-fix beats a promote: the seam network is genuinely closed, and saying so while parking the deliverable is more useful than shipping on a green sub-metric. | QLAB-CW2 | H | **"When does error mitigation help vs. hurt?" — turning a real misfire into a map (QLAB CW-2, 2026-07-12).** Phase 3b caught error mitigation BACKFIRING on a real trapped-ion machine at 100 shots (the misfire, published in QLAB-P7). CW-2 asks the general question that misfire poses: across a grid of shot budgets × noise levels, WHEN does each mitigation method help vs. hurt? Sweep {none, ZNE, PEC} on simulated noise → a green/red heat-map. Two findings a general reader keeps: (1) **the backfire is a REGIME, not a fluke** — below ~512 shots at ≥3% noise, zero-noise-extrapolation (ZNE) makes the answer WORSE because it amplifies shot-noise into the extrapolation; above that it helps. It's a tool with a failure mode, and the map shows exactly where the mode lives. (2) **the "unbiased" method isn't free** — probabilistic error cancellation (PEC) never backfires (it's mathematically unbiased), but its sampling cost is EXPONENTIAL in circuit depth×noise: on this tiny 2-qubit circuit it already needs **892× more shots** than one raw run to hold the same error bar, vs ZNE's flat 3×. So the honest engineering choice is a trade — cheap-but-can-backfire vs never-backfires-but-explodes-in-cost — and the map makes the trade concrete instead of quoting a single prettier number (§18E "mitigation isn't free"). Content-craft: the whole map runs on a SIMULATOR (zero hardware cost) and a single tiny real-IBM job (2 QPU-seconds) confirms the low-noise corner — sim-heavy is the honest, cheap way to map a method's behavior, hardware just spot-checks it. On-brand green/red heat-map SVG (one panel per method, gold accent). Post angle: "error mitigation has a failure mode, and I mapped exactly where it is — plus why the method that can't fail costs you 900× the shots." DRAFTS ONLY; go-live owner-gated. PR MushiSenpai/qlab#10. | QLAB CW-2 build (2026-07-12) | unmined |
| QLAB-S1 | H | **I built an agent to hunt the holy grail of quantum ML — and it found the good corner stays empty (QLAB S-1, 2026-07-12) — §A-WORTHY.** The central open question of variational QML: is there a circuit that is BOTH trainable (no barren plateau) AND classically hard (a cheap classical simulator can't reproduce it)? The 2024-25 Cerezo result says essentially no — the trainable circuits are the simulable ones. S-1 turns that into a SEARCH: an LLM agent proposes circuit variants, each scored (in a sandbox) on two axes — trainability = gradient variance, hardness = classical-surrogate reproduction error — and the loop maps the trainable×hard plane. **The honest finding: the good corner is (near-)empty.** The three transferable lessons: (1) **the metric almost lied.** First cut scored hardness at a deliberately LOW surrogate budget (Pauli weight ≤ 4) and the agent triumphantly reported "trainable AND hard" 14 of 16 times — the holy grail, apparently. Re-running the SAME circuits at weight 6-8 reproduced them cheaply: the "hardness" was the surrogate being *hobbled*, not the circuit being hard. Fix = measure at the surrogate's GENEROUS best effort; the fake grail collapsed and the dilemma reappeared. Lesson: when an agentic search suddenly reports a suspiciously positive result, suspect the reward function before celebrating — a too-easy adversary turns a hard negative into a fake positive. (2) **single-surrogate "hard" is a weak certificate.** Even best-effort, one surrogate (Pauli-propagation) can call a circuit "hard" that a DIFFERENT surrogate (MPS/tensor-network) reproduces cheaply — exactly the CW-1 disagreement. So the handful of marginal survivors are CANDIDATES pending a two-surrogate re-audit, not a claim. Honesty = reporting them as candidates and naming the unrun confirming test, not banking the number. (3) **the search is structurally simulator-only** — the agent's circuit code runs in a network-less bwrap jail with no device field, so it CANNOT reach a real quantum computer or spend a cent of hardware budget; "an agent that hunts quantum advantage" and "an agent that can't touch quantum hardware" are the same sentence here. Post angle: "I built an AI agent to search for the holy grail of quantum ML; here's the map it drew and why the good corner stays empty — and the moment its own reward function nearly fooled me." | QLAB-S2 | H | **The SOTA fix for my broken quantum kernel worked — and it still lost to a 2-line classical baseline (QLAB S-2, 2026-07-12) — §A-WORTHY.** Earlier (Phase 4b) I measured a "fidelity" quantum kernel CONCENTRATE: as I added qubits, every pair of data points started looking identical to it (the off-diagonal similarity variance collapsed ~64× from 2 to 10 qubits), so the kernel literally couldn't tell points apart — and it lost to a classical RBF-SVM. The known SOTA answer is the **projected quantum kernel** (Huang et al., *Power of data in QML*, 2021): instead of comparing the full exponentially-thin quantum states, you PROJECT each state down to a small fixed classical fingerprint — the per-qubit Pauli expectations {⟨X⟩,⟨Y⟩,⟨Z⟩} — and compare those. Because that fingerprint lives in a fixed low-dimensional classical space, it shouldn't concentrate. **It worked exactly as advertised — and that's the honest, deflating part.** Same feature map, only the read-out changed, so it's a clean apples-to-apples test: the projected kernel's similarity variance barely moved (1.8× vs the fidelity kernel's 63.7× collapse — a **35× resistance ratio**), and its points stayed distinguishable (mean similarity ~0.31) while the fidelity kernel's collapsed to 0.014 (everything identical). **BUT it still doesn't beat the classical RBF** (two-moons 0.88 vs 0.97; MNIST-0/1 0.73 vs 1.00). The lesson a general reader keeps: **curing a pathology is not the same as winning.** Resisting concentration is *necessary but not sufficient* for a quantum advantage — the SOTA fix does precisely what the paper promises at the level it promises it (the kernel stays healthy as it scales) and the honest bottom line is still "classical wins here." Two content-craft points: (1) the strongest honest QML story isn't "quantum loses," it's "I applied the field's own recommended fix, it did its job, and the classical baseline was STILL better" — that's more credible and more interesting than a naive loss; (2) the fair-comparison discipline is the whole game — same feature map, same Gaussian-bandwidth heuristic the RBF uses (no cherry-picked tuning), same splits — so neither kernel is rigged to win or lose. On-brand SVG: two concentration curves on a log axis, the fidelity kernel diving, the projected kernel holding flat (red vs gold). Simulator-only, zero hardware cost. Post angle: "the recommended fix for my broken quantum kernel worked perfectly — and it still lost to a two-line classical baseline." DRAFTS ONLY; go-live owner-gated. | QLAB-S3 | H | **Everything that "escapes" the quantum dead-zone just teleports you back to the classical world (QLAB S-3, 2026-07-12) — §A-WORTHY.** Earlier (Phase 4) I measured the "barren plateau": as a quantum ML circuit gets bigger, the training signal (the gradient) vanishes exponentially — at 14 qubits it had shrunk **1436×**, so the model is untrainable at any real scale. The field has a shelf of proposed ESCAPE techniques, and S-3 tests four of them honestly on the SAME setup, fitting the same decay rate α under each: start the parameters tiny (small-angle init), start the circuit as a do-nothing identity (identity-block init), grow the circuit one shallow layer at a time (layerwise training), and switch to a "local" cost that only looks at two qubits. **The honest, nuanced result — and this is the whole story:** some techniques DO keep the gradients alive, but there's always a catch. Small-angle and identity-block flatten the decay almost perfectly (α drops from +0.64 to +0.01 / −0.14) — **but only at the STARTING point**; they keep the signal alive at the top of the hill, and say nothing about whether it survives once training walks you down. Layerwise training **doesn't beat the plateau at all** on the width axis (α +0.66, essentially the baseline). And the one technique that RELIABLY keeps gradients alive at every size — the local cost — is exactly the regime that a companion result (S-1) showed a classical computer can simulate cheaply. **So the punchline: every genuine escape from the untrainable "barren plateau" lands you back in the region where a classical computer could have done the job anyway.** You can have trainable, or you can have hard-for-classical, but on this setup you can't have both — escaping the dead-zone teleports you back to the classical world. The transferable lessons: (1) **"keeps gradients alive" is a trap phrase** — three of four techniques qualify by SOME reading, and a naive write-up would trumpet "we beat barren plateaus"; the honest cut separates "alive at the starting line" from "alive during the whole race," and this experiment only measures the starting line (stated as a caveat, not buried). (2) **the negative result is the product** — reporting which escapes are real, which are init-only mirages, and which are just the easy/classical regime in disguise is far more useful than forcing a win. On-brand SVG: five decay curves on a log axis — the baseline and layerwise diving, small-angle/identity/local holding flat (gold = the plateau). Reused the Phase-4 measurement UNCHANGED (the baseline reproduced α=0.6385 to the digit — proof it's the same ruler). Simulator-only, zero hardware cost. Post angle: "the quantum-ML dead-zone has famous escape routes; I tested four, and every one that works just drops you back where a classical computer already wins." DRAFTS ONLY; go-live owner-gated. | QLAB-S4 | H | **I reproduced a published quantum-advantage result exactly — then erased it three ways (QLAB S-4, 2026-07-12) — §A-WORTHY, the capstone of the honest-QML series.** The most-cited "quantum advantage in machine learning" result (Huang et al., *Power of data in QML*, Nature Communications 2021) is subtler than its headline. Its real claim: given a quantum kernel and a classical kernel, you can CONSTRUCT a labeling of a real dataset (via the "geometric difference" g) so the quantum kernel separates it while the classical one is stuck near a coin-flip. S-4 reproduces that construction FAITHFULLY on PCA-reduced MNIST — measured g=716, and the projected quantum kernel (PQK) hits 0.900 while the "weak" classical kernel it was rigged against gets 0.730, a real +0.17 engineered edge. **Then I erase it three ways on the SAME data:** (1) a **fairly-tuned classical sweep** (grid-search RBF-SVM + gradient boosting + MLP) doesn't just close the gap — it BEATS the PQK, 0.910 vs 0.900 (Huang's ~chance classical is the ONE fixed kernel that entered g; a fairly-tuned one is the honest comparison, per Bowles & Schuld 2024, "the subtle art of benchmarking QML"); (2) **Random Fourier Features dequantization** — the PQK is a Gaussian kernel on a fixed classical fingerprint, exactly what RFF approximates classically — matches it to the digit, 0.900 vs 0.900, so the "quantum" kernel was classically reproducible; (3) **device noise** (a reduced-qubit depolarization + finite-shot model) drags the PQK down toward the classical line. **The honest bottom line: the advantage is real but MANUFACTURED — it lives in the labels, not in nature — and it collapses the moment you compare fairly.** The transferable lessons: (1) **reproduce faithfully, THEN critique** — the credible move isn't "quantum loses," it's "I rebuilt the exact famous result, confirmed its number, and showed what it does and doesn't mean"; (2) **a benchmark tuned to favour a method measures the benchmark, not the method** — engineered separations are legitimate math and illegitimate marketing; (3) **the null result had its own trap** — my first noise model (uniform depolarization) did nothing, because a Gaussian kernel with a re-fit bandwidth is scale-invariant; the effect that actually corrupts the engineered edge is finite-shot SCATTER, not the average contraction (found + fixed in-session, stated in the code so no one re-derives the null). On-brand SVG: the five-bar "erase-it-three-ways" chart with a chance line — PQK gold, the erasers closing it. Reused the S-2 projected-kernel module UNCHANGED (only added the g-relabeling + the three erasers). Simulator-only, CPU, ZERO Open-Quantum spend (spec Budget policy — an engineered edge only gets worse on real hardware, demonstrable for free); the optional IBM leg is an Aer noise confirmation, no job submitted. Post angle: "I reproduced the most-cited quantum-ML advantage exactly — then erased it three ways: a fair classical baseline beat it, random Fourier features matched it, and noise removed the rest." R-2 tests that on the richest data I have: text turned into vectors by a real language-model embedder (`all-MiniLM-L6-v2`, 384 numbers per document), classifying baseball posts vs space posts. The pipeline: embed the text → squeeze to a handful of dimensions with PCA → feed those into a quantum kernel (both flavours — the fidelity/overlap kernel and the projected kernel my earlier posts built) → compare head-to-head against a plain classical model on the SAME data. **The result is a clean, useful negative:** a boring classical model (logistic regression / RBF-SVM) on the full embedding scores **0.99** — the language model already did the hard work, the two topics are nearly a straight line apart in embedding space. Both quantum kernels score **0.48–0.56** — a coin flip. The quantum feature map doesn't just fail to help, it actively DESTROYS the structure: cramming 384 rich dimensions down to 6–10 qubits throws away exactly what made the data separable, and the fidelity kernel's similarity scores collapse into indistinguishable mush as you add qubits (the "kernel concentration" pathology from my earlier posts, showing up again on real data). **Transferable lessons:** (1) **quantum ML's problem isn't the quantum part, it's the data bottleneck** — you can only load a few numbers per qubit, so on already-rich representations the encoding is pure loss; (2) **the honest negative is the content** — "I connected the two most-hyped things in my stack (LLMs + quantum) and measured that together they're worse than either alone" is a more credible post than a forced win; (3) **a precondition that turns out false is itself the finding** — the original plan was to serve the embeddings through my local LLM router (LiteLLM), but checking first revealed it serves no embedding model at all, so I used a local CPU embedder and documented the deviation rather than build on a broken assumption. Every number traces to a CSV; the classical baseline sits in the same table as the quantum one (no cherry-picking); simulator-only, CPU, zero hardware cost. On-brand SVG: the five-bar chart, classical bars near the top, quantum bars stuck at the chance line. Post angle: "I gave a quantum computer a language model's homework — PCA'd embeddings into a quantum kernel — and a plain classical model beat it by 40 points; the quantum step was pure information loss." Lessons with legs: (1) **a scalar proxy can bless a change the real evaluator rejects** — the rig was validated in Phase 1 on frame-mean-luma "in band" (97.5→109), but when I actually ran the JUDGE against re-rendered calibration anchors (the step the predecessor skipped), the bright rig made drift WORSE (2→4): it darkened a near-miss into a 0 and blew a matte-clay hole-test into a featureless white blob the judge scored a perfect 10. Calibrate on the real evaluator, not a proxy that correlates with it. (2) **a bright metal-rig is the WRONG light for matte/clay anchors** — clay albedo ~1.0 under a bright world clips to white and HIDES the very holes the anchor exists to test; the rig invariant "render anchors under the operating rig" breaks when one anchor is a different material class. (3) **verify your "known-good" anchors by eye** — the flagged "false-zero" helmet anchor turned out to be a genuinely broken asset (dark, torn, no gold/reliefs, nothing like its reference); the judge's 0 was CORRECT and the anchor was mislabeled. A good-band anchor must be a genuinely good render. (4) **root-cause the missing call, don't add a new pass** — the residual speckle existed because the synthesis code path MEASURED speckle but never called the de-speckle function that already existed in a sibling path; the fix was one line wiring, not new machinery. (5) **de-speckling INTERIORS doesn't touch the SILHOUETTE** — after the interior chips were gone, the judge still saw "torn/exposed framework" because the ragged black-bleed + white chips live at the object's OUTLINE (UV-island edges + 2K-downres halo), a different defect class the interior de-speckle and a wider gutter don't reach. | OPS-133 | H | **A hybrid that finally combines two half-wins — and the honest residual is a tone-fidelity-vs-legibility tension, not a bug (3D F115 projection+Paint hybrid, 2026-07-12).** Two texture approaches each won half the problem: photo-projection nails faithful pixels + crisp edges + correct metal/dielectric split on the faces the camera saw, but has nothing for the unseen ~70%; a generative paint model (Hunyuan3D-Paint 2.1) fills the whole mesh coherently but is over-metallic (paints black carbon fibre as metal=1) and chalky-white. (2) **don't trust a generative model's PBR channels — re-derive them from your own semantics** — the paint metallic map was mean 0.97 (physically impossible for a carbon-dielectric object); discarding it and rebuilding metallic/roughness from the geometry-driven material layout took the physics gate from advisory-fail to a clean pass (metal fraction 0.97→0.51, the impossible-dark-metal count 51→0). (4) **the real trap: "faithful to a dark reference" reads as "too dark to see the material" under neutral light** — the tone-matched crate is genuinely faithful to the reference's dark tone and passes every deterministic gate, but under a plain game rig it reads near-monochrome; the carbon/steel/LED distinction that pops under a studio hero rig doesn't survive to a neutral render. That's not a defect to fix silently — it's a tone-fidelity-vs-legibility CHOICE that belongs to the owner, so the honest move is to stage both the faithful result and the well-lit hero and let them pick. (5) **a gate can regress from unrelated infra and fail an innocent asset** — the shipped-vs-QA silhouette gate started failing at 0.81 not because of holes but because a recent "industry gates" change added witness calibration objects (grey/chrome balls + colour chips) to the shipped render only; those extra blobs inflated the silhouette mask. (5) **GIF hygiene**: ffmpeg palettegen `stats_mode=full` + `paletteuse=dither=none` on flat pastel art is smaller AND cleaner than bayer dithering (516K vs 580K); to eyeball the true last frame you must `convert -coalesce` first (GIF delta-frames extract as near-blank otherwise — that blank is optimization, not a bad clip). Result: 720×405, 524KB, full arc empty→stack→collapse banner, committed pathspec-scoped past 25 untouched OWNER-gated staged files, pushed main, verified raw GIF HTTP 200 on GitHub. | komorebi H-3 gameplay GIF (commit 154d27c, 2026-07-13) | unmined |
| OPS-135 | H | **The rights-clean sample render forced the two-pass VRAM dance and surfaced a real coexistence bug (comic-narrator H-1, 2026-07-13).** Rendered the first PUBLIC sample video for comic-manga-narrator from a CC-BY Pepper & Carrot page (ep02 "Rainbow potions" P01 — hi-res per-page JPGs live at `peppercarrot.com/0_sources/<ep>/hi-res/en_..._EnnPnn.jpg`; the directory index is browsable, guessing the URL pattern 404s so `curl` the listing). Lessons with legs: (1) **agent-mode's "Nemotron-light + audio coexist" claim breaks when Fish Speech grabs VRAM before vLLM profiles** — the agent vLLM crashed `ValueError: No available memory for the cache blocks` because `creative-tts` (Fish) eager-loaded ~2.2GB and vLLM's `--gpu-memory-utilization 0.82` then had negative KV headroom; fix = start vLLM on a CLEAN card, THEN bring Fish back (reorder, don't co-start). The mode script starts them together and loses this race. (2) **IndexTTS-2 (~8GB) does NOT co-fit with the light agent vLLM (~26GB) on 32GB**, so the honest full-quality path is the documented TWO-PASS: `--vision-only` writes page.json/script.json against vLLM, then FREE vLLM, start IndexTTS-2 :9012, resume with `--from-page-json/--from-script-json` (no vision GPU in phase 3-4). Single-pass only works with Fish. (3) **IndexTTS-2's BigVGAN CUDA-kernel JIT fails on this toolchain (`nvcc fatal: Unsupported gpu architecture 'compute_70'`) but it's NON-FATAL** — the server logs "Falling back to torch" and comes up `{"status":"ok","loaded":true}`; a monitor grepping "Error" false-alarms on it, so gate readiness on the health endpoint, not a log-error match. (4) **the auto-mode classifier blocks `MUSHISHI_ASSUME_YES=1` on a mode script** (it can't see the guard's IDLE proof) — but agent-mode/forensic-mode have NO interactive prompt anyway (only the mode-PICKER calls `confirm_or_assume`), so the env var was a no-op; run the script plainly after verifying IDLE via `gpu_busy_report` (empty = idle) yourself. (5) **restore = teardown-first, then the ORIGINAL resident** — stopped everything I started (IndexTTS-2 host PID, creative-tts, worker) to a clean card so `forensic-mode.sh` had nothing to stop (skips its confirm prompt), then it restored `vllm-nemotron` to the exact before-state (29646 vs 29836 MiB, health=healthy restarts=0). (6) **this page has no dialogue** so its audio is the ambient/SFX/music bed, not spoken voices — stated verbatim in the README so the claim matches what ran. (7) **`docs/*.mp4` was gitignored** by the global `*.mp4` outputs rule → added an explicit `!docs/sample/` un-ignore rather than force-add, so the intent is documented. Result: 2.76MB MP4 (1920×1080, 9.3s) + 5MB GIF, README "Sample output" section, commit 38bac4f, pushed main, raw GIF/MP4 HTTP 200 on GitHub; gitleaks clean. | comic-narrator H-1 rights-clean sample (commit 38bac4f, 2026-07-13) | unmined |
| OPS-137 | H | **The showcase render needed ZERO GPU, and the honest B3 was "two geometries, not three" once I read the manifests instead of the dir names (3d-stack H-2, 2026-07-13).** Added a hero turntable GIF + a partial B3 quality panel to the public 3d-stack repo. Lessons with legs: (1) **the turntable is a CPU job — don't touch the tenant** — the meshops pipeline already ships a `turntable.py` (Blender CPU Cycles, "zero-VRAM friendly") with a production `shipped_rig` (3-point key/fill/rim sun + bright gradient world) built specifically so metals read as metal not a flat-grey blob (the estate flood-lit-metal lesson, baked into the rig); I ran it in a `--rm mushishi-meshops` throwaway container mounting `/app`+outputs, GPU never switched, vLLM held its 29GB untouched start-to-finish. Reuse the pipeline's own render stage, don't hand-roll lighting. (2) **KTX2/basisu delivery LODs won't import in the container's Blender** (`KHR_texture_basisu not available`) — the polished B4 hero LOD0 is KTX2-compressed (its 6MB-vs-26MB win); fell back to the real TRELLIS.2 `s2-raw/model.glb` (WebP `EXT_texture_webp`, which DOES load) for the same swordsmith helmet. Check `extensionsUsed` before picking the render input. (3) **dir names lie; the manifest `stages[stage==S2].tool` is ground truth** — the `*-swordsmith-hero-d3s2-paint` job dirs look like Direct3D-S2 deliveries but their S2 `tool=fixture-mock`/`qa=waived` = STUBS reusing a cache mesh; the only REAL non-TRELLIS gens live in a one-off experiment cache (`gilder-e2e/`), where the hero was built D3-S2 shape → Hunyuan paint (chained, confirmed via the comfyui graph JSON's `Hy3DLoadMesh` on the d3s2 GLB). (4) **so the truthful B3 is TWO distinct geometries, not three** — TRELLIS.2 (real, swordsmith input, 195k tris) vs D3-S2 (150k) — and Hunyuan is a TEXTURE PASS over the identical D3-S2 geometry (byte-identical silhouette + tris confirmed it), on a DIFFERENT input than TRELLIS. Shipped it as a partial panel (shaded+silhouette rows, per-leg tris/texture/coverage CSV) with a gold-bordered "Scope & honesty" block stating exactly that, and KEPT the COVERAGE.md B3-pending flag for the real same-input run — the work-order's "2-of-3, labelled, keep the flag" path. Don't force a fake 3-way when the disambiguating manifest read says it isn't one. (5) **media un-ignore was a no-op here** — the repo's gitignore only blocks `*.log`/`*.bak`, PNGs already tracked, so no `!` needed (unlike H-1's `*.mp4`); still verified `git check-ignore` before assuming. Result: 720px 36-frame GIF (2.78MB) + panel PNG (241KB) + CSV + b3/README, commit 2803742, pushed master, all raw URLs HTTP 200; gitleaks clean; GPU before==after (vllm-nemotron 29368MiB, healthy). | 3d-stack H-2 turntable + partial B3 (commit 2803742, 2026-07-13) | unmined |

## Site redesign: conversion pass from first outside feedback (SITE, 2026-07-12)
| id | pri | lesson / decision | source | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SITE-7 | H | **First outside review said the nav read as decoration — the visitor thought the site was a SPA and never clicked the tabs.** Muted mono links on the brand's baseline with no divider = invisible chrome. Fix: hairline under the nav + gold underline on the active tab. Lesson: a nav that only insiders recognize as a nav fails its one job; test IA on someone who has never seen the site. | condo-friend feedback via owner | unmined |
| SITE-8 | H | **A case-study wall buries the blog.** Nine full Problem/Solution/Outcome cards sat between the /blog h1 and the post list; skimmers never reached the posts. Fix: compact outcome-first case cards (one bolded metric each) + a sticky CSS-only jumpbar (case studies / failure log / topics / field guide). Rule of thumb: on a page with two audiences, neither may need to scroll past the other's content. | condo-friend feedback via owner | unmined |
| SITE-9 | M | **CTA must be one click from the proof, not nested behind services.** Every case card and service card now carries a prefilled-subject mailto; hero and page-ends carry a contact band (the ∅ mark's single on-page use, owner-approved). Conversion feedback: "with each use case allow someone to click directly to get in touch." | condo-friend feedback via owner | unmined |
| SITE-10 | M | **Light theme without breaking the zero-JS brand: prefers-color-scheme only.** Tokens redefined in one @media block in global.css :root (accent deepened #e8a33d→#8f5e13 for AA on paper), paired theme-color metas in Base.astro, `color-scheme` set for native form controls. No toggle, no localStorage, no FOUC. Standalone public/*.html pages keep their own heads — adapt separately if ever needed. | owner choice (friend asked for light theme) | unmined |
| SITE-11 | M | **The homepage sections were status-driven (building/shipped) but never recency-driven — the newest, strongest work (QLAB) wasn't even a project entry.** Now: "Latest work" = top-3 by `started` date (self-maintaining as entries are added), everything else collapses to compact rows. A portfolio homepage's first section should answer "what did this person do LAST month," not "what is their oldest stable thing." | owner request | unmined |
| SITE-12 | L | **Owner writing rule: no em dashes in site copy** (AI-writing tell per outside feedback); codified in repo CLAUDE.md, applied to all page copy (blog posts left for opportunistic cleanup). `<title>` separator exempt. | condo-friend feedback via owner | unmined |
| OPS-120 | H | **Moving a credential into a file breaks every consumer that grepped it out of another script.** ntfy topic moved from an inline literal in healthcheck.sh to ~/.config/mushishi-infra/ntfy.topic; three scripts (benchmark.sh, saas-idea-pipeline.sh, 3d-watchlist-sweep.sh) still grepped healthcheck.sh for `NTFY_TOPIC="..."` and silently extracted the literal string `$(cat .../ntfy.topic)` — their alerts went to a dead topic. Fixed 2026-07-13: all consumers now read the topic FILE. Lesson: when a credential's source-of-truth moves, grep the estate for every extraction pattern of the OLD source, not just direct references. | ntfy topic migration 2026-07-12 | unmined |
| OPS-121 | H | **`systemctl --user` is dead under cron (no user D-Bus), and a 2>/dev/null on it turns that into silent false data.** estate-brain.sh swallowed "Failed to connect to bus", got an EMPTY timer list, and daily-nagged "timer NOT scheduled" for two perfectly healthy timers (obsidian-sync, launchpadlib-cache-clean) for days. Fixed 2026-07-13: export XDG_RUNTIME_DIR + DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS before the call. Lesson: any cron script calling `systemctl --user` needs the user-bus env exported, and a monitoring script must distinguish "empty result" from "query failed". | estate-brain daily digest false alerts | unmined |
| OPS-122 | M | **Manual/parallel-session publishes must flip the queue.md row, or blog-bot will re-draft a live post.** The 2026-06-22 C4 case-study session published the sam3-vace and avatar-pipeline posts but left both rows `[queued]`; sam3 was FIRST in the queue, so the next bot run would have re-generated an already-published slug (overwriting the live post). Rows flipped 2026-07-13. Same silent-skip class as OPS-1/64/66: the publish isn't done until every index/ledger that tracks it is updated. | queue audit 2026-07-13 | unmined |
| OPS-138 | H | **Recording a LIVE failover demo means fighting three invisible actors: sudo, the idle-judge, and your own timeout (sovereign-stack H-5, 2026-07-13).** Recorded the routing-failover GIF (GPU sovereign :8000 → CPU floor :8001 → restore) for the public sovereign-ai-stack README. Lessons with legs: (1) **an estate script's `sudo x || true` hangs a recorded/headless session forever** — forensic-mode.sh's optional `sudo nvidia-smi -pl 450` prompted on the xterm TTY (redirects don't cover /dev/tty) and parked take-1 at step 6 for 6 minutes; fix WITHOUT editing the estate script = exported bash function `sudo(){ command sudo -n "$@" 2>/dev/null || true; }; export -f sudo` so the child script's sudo fails fast into its own `|| true`. (2) **the tier-proof for a router failover demo is the `x-litellm-model-api-base` + `x-litellm-attempted-fallbacks` response headers** — the JSON body's `model` field echoes the alias (`sovereign-only`) on every tier, so it proves nothing; the headers show :8000 vs :8001 and 0 vs 2 fallbacks on screen. (3) **gpu-idle-judge (cron */10) auto-restores the canonical tenant ~10s after any stop** — it, not my demo's restore step, brought vllm-nemotron back in take 1 (stopped 18:20:0x, judge up'd it 18:20:02, healthy 75s later); a deliberate-outage demo must be timed against the judge's cron slots and its restore treated as a feature (the outage self-heals in <90s even if the operator does nothing). (4) **my own `timeout 400` killed the recorder mid-take** — budget recording wall-time = demo + model reload (75-120s) + GIF encode, and a TERM-killed x11grab MP4 was still ffprobe-valid (salvageable), so probe before re-shooting. (5) **xterm rows × font height must fit the Xvfb framebuffer** — 46 rows at fs11 ≈ 782px > 760px screen, so the bottom 1-2 lines (closing summary) were clipped out of frame; compute geometry from the framebuffer, not vice versa. (6) **Nemotron reasoning models return `content: null` + `reasoning_content` unless the system message is `detailed thinking off`** — a demo that shows `None` as the answer looks broken; and budget max_tokens ≥96 for the CPU leg or `finish_reason: length` eats the content. (7) **honest GIF editing = announce the cut on screen** — the ~70s reload wait is trimmed but the demo prints "that wait is cut from this GIF, timer shown below" AND the measured "serving again after 71s" line, so the edit is disclosed inside the artifact itself. Result: 49s 3.3MB GIF, README embed with dated caption, commit fcd7b80, pushed main, raw URL HTTP 200; gitleaks clean; tenant restored healthy (29650MiB, `sovereign-only` → :8000, fallbacks 0). | sovereign-stack H-5 failover recording (commit fcd7b80, 2026-07-13) | unmined |
| OPS-139 | H | **Stage-P is TWO deliverables and shipping one re-creates the publish≠reachable bug at repo scale (QLAB flip, 2026-07-14).** The Honest-QML-Lab case study + posts went live pointing at github.com/MushiSenpai/qlab for reproducibility — while the repo was still PRIVATE: every visitor got a 404 on the page's strongest credibility claim. Root cause = QLAB Stage-P (spec §17: public repo + posts, gated on ≥2 credible reports + clean sweeps + owner timing) was executed HALFWAY — posts shipped, repo-flip forgotten; the owner-timing gate was implicitly passed the moment the posts linked the repo. Fix: pre-flip prep commit (MIT LICENSE so replication is actually permitted — a public repo without a license does NOT deliver "reproducible"; public CLAUDE.md policy keeping the no-advantage-claims discipline; stale "drafts only" README line), gitleaks full-history clean, then flip + anonymous verification (repo page 200, unauth API 200, anonymous clone, raw CDN after its 404-cache TTL — raw.githubusercontent caches 404s ~5min and ignores query-string busting, so pre-flip probes poison the first post-flip checks). Lessons with legs: (1) any publish that LINKS an artifact must curl-verify the artifact anonymously, not just the page; (2) a "public repo" promise includes a license; (3) check every case study/post for links to private repos as part of the publish checklist. | QLAB Stage-P completion (2026-07-14) | unmined |
| 3D-INTEGRATE3 | M | **"Blocked" records rot into false mechanisms — re-diagnose before you re-derive scope (integrate3 three-model S2 integration, 2026-07-14, F117).** Integrated Pixal3D :9110 / Hi3DGen :9111 / TripoSG+PartCrafter :9112 as per-service stack tenants. Lessons with legs: (1) **PartCrafter's "SPEC-BLOCKED-UNTIL-TRIPOSG" label was wrong on the mechanism** — the block was never licensing (both MIT) and never a TripoSG-import/download dep (PartCrafter vendors TripoSGVAEModel in its own src/, weights self-contained). The REAL block was a missing `diso` CUDA pkg (GitHub #19) + a `torch_cluster.fps` import at VAE module-load. A stale blocker note that names the wrong cause costs a session re-deriving a false prerequisite — read the actual ImportError, don't trust the label. (2) **`diso`/`torch_cluster` are CUDA extensions whose setup.py gates the GPU build on `torch.cuda.is_available()` — which is FALSE in a docker build** → they silently compile a CPU-only stub that ImportErrors at runtime (`undefined symbol ...CUDualMC...`). Fix = `FORCE_CUDA=1` + `TORCH_CUDA_ARCH_LIST=12.0` + CUDA headers on `CPATH` (the c++ compile of pybind.cpp needs `cuda_runtime.h`). This is the F54 sm_120 build class for ANY torch-CUDAExtension built in a GPU-less docker layer. (3) **consume a parallel trial's artifacts, don't duplicate** — the Pixal3D trial had already built the sm_120 NATTEN image + downloaded 26GB of weights to the shared HF cache; FROMing its `pixal3d:ready` image + pointing MODEL_PATH at the cache snapshot reduced the Pixal3D service to a thin FastAPI shell. (4) **bypass the fragile dep, don't fix it, when a bypass exists** — Pixal3D's MoGe camera estimator dies on a `utils3d.pt` namespace drift; `--fov` (manual FOV) skips MoGe entirely = the robust path (the trial separately added an alias shim, so both work now). (5) **reproduce the baseline metric before quoting a comparison** — the Hi3DGen "machined not sculpted" claim needs the SAME hard-edge metric (dihedral>30° via trimesh face_adjacency_angles) run on the TRELLIS.2 baseline; mine reproduced the documented 29,217 exactly, so the Hi3DGen number is apples-to-apples not a re-labelled statistic. | integrate3 3-model S2 integration (2026-07-14) | unmined |
| 3D-PIXAL3D | M | **A "single-photo de-lit PBR" model can genuinely beat your pipeline on ONE axis while losing the deliverable — measure the axis, don't buy the headline (Pixal3D trial, 2026-07-14).** Trialed TencentARC/Pixal3D (MIT, single image→mesh+PBR via pixel-back-projection) vs our crate chain. It DID close the "no proven de-lit PBR from one photo" gap: its neural albedo measured lowfreq-luminance-std 0.071 vs our projection-hybrid's 0.169 (ours bakes the photo's real lighting; its is genuinely de-lit). But it lost the deliverable on everything else: 990k raw tris (not game retopo), metallic=1.0 EVERYWHERE (zero dielectric segmentation — worse than our carbon-vs-steel split), and wavy "sculpted" panel edges that do NOT fix the owner's machined-edge complaint (that's a geometry-stage model's job, not a texture model's). Verdict = texture-stage DONOR (its de-lit albedo onto our mesh), not a replacement generator. Lessons with legs: (1) a fair model trial measures the SPECIFIC gap it claims (de-lit → F111 lowfreq-L-std on the albedo map, apples-to-apples against your own map by the same method), not a vibe from a hero render; (2) reuse the estate's already-built sm_120 base image when the new model shares a backbone — Pixal3D rides TRELLIS.2, so building on td-trellis2:p5 sidestepped the whole native-kernel gauntlet and reduced the trial to one gate (NATTEN source build, which passed); (3) the README's "~18GB peak" was for the non-cascade path — the real 1536 cascade OOM'd 32GB, low_vram fit at 25.6GB; trust the measurement, not the doc. | Pixal3D image-to-3D trial (2026-07-14) | unmined |

- (2026-07-14, diag-20260714) LESSON — "unseen ≠ inventable": a symmetric hard-surface asset's unseen faces must INHERIT their symmetric counterpart, not fall to a flat material base. The crate's white end-caps were `_run_hybrid` filling ±Y faces (zero projection, zero mirror) with STEEL_RGB metallic 1.0 → blown white under the shipped rig. Fix = mirror across EVERY symmetry axis the S4 metrics support (crate was XYZ-symmetric; the old path only mirrored the single best axis). Also: a "delivered" build can be a re-staged QUARANTINED build — checksum delivery/ against delivery-rejected/ before diagnosing texture, and measure the defect (per-view white/black fraction, top-face symmetry offset in mm) rather than eyeballing.

- (2026-07-14, compass2mv-20260714) LESSON — synthesized multi-view is a PROPORTION cure, not a detail cure: on the client crate, QIE-Multiple-Angles→Hunyuan3D-2mv beat single-image TRELLIS on bbox-proportion fidelity (mean rel-err 0.095 vs 0.122) and gave CONTROL of the unseen back (TRELLIS cloned the front onto the back, latches and all; 2mv followed its conditioned back view) — but 2mv's raw geometry is ~7× softer on machined hard edges (2,640 vs 17,199 dihedral>40°). Pick per failure mode: proportion/back bugs → multi-view; edge-crispness bugs → single-image + retopo. Also: a coordination "lock file" may be an flock, not an existence flag — poll the flock (try to acquire), not the file, and HOLD it for your whole GPU window so idle automations stand down; and any image-space feature gate must mask background by corner-distance, not luminance windows (grey synth backgrounds read as "metal" and false-reject).

### §B — 3D convergence rebuild lesson (2026-07-14, F120)
- Stacking measured winners on ONE build beats iterating a single lane: proportion-exact
  geometry (2mv + anisotropic scale, rel-err 0.000) is the ROOT-CAUSE cure for texture
  registration offset (features can only co-register when the mesh matches the reference
  proportions) — the F118 "moved sideways" complaint dropped -90mm→-29mm from this alone.
- Multi-axis symmetry mirror + a real de-lit donor (Pixal3D) turns white unseen panels
  (57-59% non-material) into 98% real material — no flat-material fallback survives.
- The deterministic G17 L1 silhouette-IoU gate compares a turntable ORBIT to a fixed 3/4
  photo → structurally low IoU (0.578) that is POSE mismatch, not a mesh defect (hole_frac
  0.0, G16 pass). It correctly owner-gates the public profile; it does not mean the asset
  is wrong. The vision judge (nemotron-omni, voted) PASSED matches_reference median 10.
- GPU etiquette win: the ENTIRE meshops chain runs CPU (S7 bake via force+CUDA-hidden) so
  a resident forensic model is never evicted — CPU shipped-rig renders replace UE when the
  card must stay free.

### §B — feature-anchored registration: test the hypothesis before you "fix" it (2026-07-14, F121)
- The owner's symptom named a cause ("yaw misregistration — the blue strip rotated off
  centre"). MEASURE the named cause before building a fix: locating every blue-emissive
  feature in BOTH the reference and the mesh and fitting a single azimuthal rotation gave
  a +0.1° yaw — the yaw hypothesis was REFUTED. The projection was already registered; the
  real defect was elsewhere. Building a "feature-anchored yaw solve" as the primary fix
  would have been solving a non-problem. A cheap deterministic measurement redirected the
  whole fix.
- The actual root cause: a symmetry MIRROR meant to fill unseen-face MATERIAL was also
  copying the glowing BLUE EMISSIVE strips onto every symmetric face (4× over-replication,
  72 vs 18 features). Emissive light is VIEW-EVIDENCE, not a mirror-able material — it must
  be locked only where the camera actually saw it. Fix = exclude emissive from the mirror
  source pool + lock it from the pre-mirror projected set only. Off the solved hero pose
  (a gallery front-on view) the over-replicated blue is what read as "the strip moved."
- A near-symmetric object has a genuine front/back 180° yaw AMBIGUITY under a silhouette
  solve: two opposite azimuths give the same silhouette, so the solver lands on either
  run-to-run. A feature-anchor can only break it when the object is asymmetric enough; on
  a geometrically front/back-symmetric crate BOTH azimuths are equally valid registrations
  — the texture is self-consistent either way, and only the render-rig's fixed hero azimuth
  needs to face the chosen side. Honest to say "not a defect, a framing consequence."
- Judge the SHIPPED pipeline frames, not scratch renders: a low-sample UV-mesh render with
  ragged-rim speckle flipped the VLM from matches_reference 10 to 0/"damaged" on an
  otherwise-identical asset. Same lesson as F115/F120; the VLM confabulates defects off
  render artifacts the deterministic gates prove absent (hole_frac 0.0, G16 pass).

### §B — the "blues disappeared" bug was a GALLERY-FRAMING bug, not a texture bug (2026-07-14, F122)
- The owner said "the blues disappeared." The blues were IN the texture — but the gallery/
  library thumbnail + turntable open on a FIXED mesh-frame view (frame 0 = camera on +X),
  which for this crate is a blank END CAP. The textured featured face faced away by default.
  When a client reviews the DEFAULT ORBIT, "verified the texture is correct on the solved
  hero pose" is NOT done — the default view must be MADE to face the featured face. Derive a
  gallery-front azimuth from the solved camera and pass it to the turntable start angle so
  the thumbnail + orbit open on the evidence-bearing face. (Rendering the actual gallery view
  before shipping would have caught all three prior rounds.)
- Single-photo→mesh registration is FUNDAMENTALLY under-constrained, and WORSE under object
  symmetry: silhouette-IoU is INVARIANT to the 180° front/back flip by construction, so it
  can NEVER pick the right face — you MUST add an appearance term (render both candidate
  poses, score by feature/photometric agreement = the FoundPose/MegaPose render-and-compare
  principle). The flip margin on a symmetric crate measured 0.2–2.4% = near coin-flip; be
  honest that crisp registration + zeroed roll needs dense-correspondence+PnP (RoMa/LoFTR)
  or 2–3 photos (multi-view), and that the ideal ML stack (torch/DINOv2/RoMa) may not fit a
  numpy-only pipeline container without a heavy rebuild + sm_120 risk. Cite the limit; don't
  keep promising a single-photo miracle.
- A disambiguator with a "only correct if it beats the current by >15%" veto DOES NOTHING on
  a symmetric object (both faces score ~equally) — it silently no-ops and leaves the result
  to solver luck. If the job is to DECIDE, hard-pick the better candidate; a self-consistent
  tie should still commit (and then align the view to whatever it committed to).
- When you can't refine a DOF honestly, don't fake it. The appearance score under-constrained
  camera ROLL — a scan drifted the tilt +12° WORSE and hit the search bound. Left roll at the
  solved value and named the correct remedy (dense-corr+PnP) rather than shipping a regression
  dressed as a fix. A worse number from a "refinement" is the tell that the objective is wrong
  for that DOF.
- Blue LEDs read as muddy dark-blue under a neutral rig unless they EMIT: an albedo-only lock
  (metallic0/rough0.25, no emission) is not enough. Write an emission map (KHR_materials_
  emissive) for the glowing features, and re-saturate the projected blue (projection averages
  a thin strip with the bright steel beside it → pale desaturated cyan). Emission is view-
  evidence: lock it only where the camera saw it, never mirror it (the F121 containment).

### §B — RoMa dense-correspondence registration + the multi-view texture lesson (2026-07-15, F123)
- Base a heavy ML container on an EXISTING working torch+cu128 image (we built td-roma:v1 on
  td-trellis2:p5) to skip the sm_120 CUDA gauntlet (F54). RoMa's compiled `local_corr` CUDA
  ext wasn't built in the image → forced the native-torch fallback (`use_custom_corr=False` on
  every decoder block) → runs on CPU (~32s/pair), which meant NEVER evicting the foreign
  resident nemotron. When a model has a "shitty native" fallback path, prefer it over a build
  gauntlet if throughput allows — quality-first ≠ GPU-first.
- Weights are licensed SEPARATELY from code, and the load-bearing question is the WEIGHTS: RoMa
  ships its OWN MIT-trained matcher weights and only fetches the DINOv2 backbone, which Meta
  RELICENSED CC-BY-NC→Apache-2.0 (2023-09-14). The historic "DINOv2 taints RoMa" fear is dead —
  but verify + ARCHIVE the actual LICENSE files before quoting a verdict; don't trust memory.
- BAKE weights into the image (F91): our first build's prefetch failed SILENTLY (WEIGHT_PREFETCH_
  PARTIAL) because that layer had no network → weights would have downloaded on first RUN. Re-
  baked from a host weight-cache via COPY and verified offline with `--network none`. "It works"
  in a `--rm` test run does NOT mean the layer persisted — the pip install evaporated with the
  container.
- The multi-view WIN is a TEXTURE win, not just a pose win: once RoMa fixes the pose, the single
  tilted 3/4 reference photo is ACTIVELY HARMFUL as a texture source — it smears colour at
  grazing angles and contaminates faces the clean per-face views cover crisply. DISCARD it and
  rebuild colour purely from the clean registered per-face views (owner's Gemini canonical sheet),
  each winning its OWN face head-on (raise the projection front-facing threshold 0.10→0.5 so a
  side view can't bleed grazing colour onto the front). projected 544k→880k texels, crisp panels.
- L1 (deterministic) beat the thing L2 (VLM) couldn't: the RoMa registration lifted G17-L1
  silhouette-IoU 0.566→0.863 = it CURED the F120-F122 game-universal quarantine. But G17-L2 voted
  still failed matches_reference 0 on SURFACE fidelity (albedo speckle from the single-photo lineage
  + retopo-softened panel edges vs a pristine reference). Per the standing rule, L2 must not overturn
  L1 on hole/IoU (L1 hole=0.0 contradicts the VLM's holes-flag = the flood-lit-metal hallucination).
  Honest partial: registration is DONE and working; the remaining gap is a geometry+texture-fidelity
  problem (Track-B re-gen + higher-res per-face bake), not a registration problem — don't conflate them.

## 2026-07-14 — Hetzner buy-attempt reality check (VPS tenancy Box 2) [§B]
- Approved sizing ≠ purchasable sizing: the tenancy decision approved "CX42-class ~€17/mo",
  but the console showed (a) the account bills USD+9% VAT (SG billing) — EUR list prices
  don't apply — and (b) a standing "Limited availability of cloud instances" incident
  (since 2026-06-26) had the ENTIRE budget line (Intel CX + ARM CAX ≥4vCPU) out of stock
  in all EU locations. In-stock alternatives ran 2.6–7× budget (CPX32 $45.77 / CPX42
  $89.37 EU, $119.89 SG). Lesson: an infra cost gate should be re-verified IN the vendor
  console (real currency, real stock) at buy time, not from cached list prices; and a
  "wait + automated restock watcher + ntfy" beats both overpaying and manual daily checks.
- Location premium is a real line item: identical CPX42 is $89.37 EU vs $119.89 Singapore
  (+34%) — and the latency argument for SG evaporates when the traffic is batch LLM
  routing (inference seconds dwarf 170ms RTT).
| OPS-134 | H | **Use the best available generator for INPUTS — sovereignty applies to what we run, not what references we accept (3D, 2026-07-15).** After 3 failed single-photo registration rounds, the owner asked a frontier image model "provide the same object from multiple sides" and got a clean 4-view sheet in seconds — better anchors than our local view-synthesis lane produced in 3.5 min of GPU choreography. The local Compass lane stays as the automated/sovereign fallback, but intake protocol now generates a 4-view sheet by the best tool available before the pipeline runs. Coordinator process miss logged: the "get more angles" recommendation should have included the 30-second frontier option from the start. |
| OPS-140 | H | **A worktree session launched from a repo SUBDIR can commit deliverables at the wrong repo-relative path — and the doc becomes invisible to every path-based check even though it IS committed (platform spec recovery, 2026-07-15).** The Mushishi Platform master spec v1.5 was "missing from disk": its session (launched from ~/Documents/business, worktree of the ~/Documents repo) wrote `platform/` at the WORKTREE ROOT, believing root = business/ — so the branch `claude/elegant-kilby-39a6bc` committed `platform/MUSHISHI-AI-PLATFORM-SPEC-v1.0.md` instead of `business/platform/...`. When the worktree was deleted, every doc citing `~/Documents/business/platform/` found nothing, and even merging the branch would have landed the file at `~/Documents/platform/` (a path nothing cites). Recovery was pure git (`git show branch:path`, sha256-verified, re-landed at the cited path, master `ab9cf90`) — no restic needed: the worktree died, the branch did not. Lessons: (1) before committing in a worktree, check where the repo root actually is (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`) vs where the session THINKS it is; (2) land estate docs to master same-session at the exact path other docs cite — a doc committed only on a worktree branch at an uncited path is the publish≠reachable bug for internal docs; (3) "missing file" triage order = git branches (`git log --all -- '*name*'`) BEFORE backup restore. | SUTRADHARA spec recovery (2026-07-15) | unmined |
| OPS-141 | H | **A "band-aid the symptom" post-pass and a "fix the classifier" root-pass produce the same map channel — but only the root fix survives a re-gate (3D DEFINITIVE crate, F125, 2026-07-15).** F123 flagged the crate "reads too chrome" and softened it with a manual metallic/roughness post-pass (roma-mat: metallic p50 255→178) — but the underlying reseg still classified 91% of texels as steel (metallic 1.0), so the very next stage that re-derives ORM from the reseg would wipe the softening. Root cause: the v3.6 STEEL-DEFAULT material classifier was tuned on the single DARK-LIT reference photo, where luma is an unreliable carbon signal (shadowed steel reads dark), so it FORBADE steel→carbon promotion — correct for that input, wrong once clean evenly-lit multi-view anchors exist. Fix = make the promotion SYMMETRIC but on a DIFFERENT signal: weave PERIODICITY (an FFT peak), which is immune to the dark-lit-steel bug that luma has. Promote only when the clean multi-view projection is the source, only on large planar outward fields, with a STRICTER peak floor than the demotion floor (34 vs 20). Result: carbon 4→13 regions, texel_frac 0.091→0.638, metallic p50→0 at the SOURCE — no post-pass needed, and it can't be undone by a re-run. Lessons with legs: (1) if a "fix" is a post-pass on a derived map, check whether the deriving stage re-runs downstream — it'll silently revert your fix (same class as OPS-109 out-of-band re-export staleness); (2) a classifier tuned on input A's failure mode becomes wrong for input B — when the input class changes (dark photo → clean multi-view), re-examine the guards that the old input justified; (3) the right way to add a promotion the old rule forbade is a DIFFERENT, bug-immune signal (FFT weave vs luma), not loosening the signal the bug lived in; (4) an emissive that "glows" needs BOTH a bright emission map AND KHR_materials_emissive_strength >1 (glTF clamps emissiveFactor to [0,1]; a bright albedo-blue alone reads as dim cyan, not an LED) — the F121/F122 "blues subdued" residual was half-fixed until the strength extension went in. | 3D definitive crate material endgame (F125, 2026-07-15) | unmined |
| OPS-142 | H | **An overcorrection reads as a NEW defect — and the cure is a REFERENCE MEASUREMENT before a bolder parameter, not a bolder parameter (3D DEFINITIVE crate BLOOMFIX, F125-addendum, 2026-07-15).** F125 fixed the "blues subdued" residual by lifting the blue LEDs to a near-white emissive floor (B≥235) + emissive_strength 5.0 — which then rendered the bars BRIGHTER than the surrounding steel and became the DOMINANT defect (large luminous white-blue smears). The measurement that reframed it: in the reference photo the blue bars are DARKER than the steel (luma 63.8 vs 174.9, ratio 0.364) — they read blue by CHROMA + contrast, not luminance; the whole "make them glow brighter" axis was pushing away from the reference. Fix = drop the floor to a saturated moderate-luminance blue (B=200) + strength 1.4, and address the real smear source: the PROJECTION painted broad BLOBS where it couldn't resolve discrete bars (4 components held 89% of emissive texels), so a blob guard collapses any over-wide component to a bar-width core. Bloom −58% emissive texels, bar width 6.1→3.88. Second half of the pass: a "durable fix marked owner-gated" was actually a ONE-LINE WIRING GAP — `_synth_brushed_steel` existed and was tested but was only called from `_run_synthesis`, never from `_run_hybrid` (the path the shipped crate uses), so steel got satin roughness but a FLAT normal = glassy; wiring an equivalent `_synth_brushed_normal()` into the hybrid path fixed it. Lessons: (1) before making a corrective parameter BOLDER, MEASURE the reference target the parameter is chasing — F125 assumed "brighter = closer" when the reference bars are dark; a ratio measurement (0.364) would have caught the inversion up front; (2) a smear/bloom is often a MASK-EXTENT problem (the emissive region is too wide) before it's an intensity problem — tighten the mask (erode + blob-guard to the feature's real geometry) before touching strength; (3) when a residual is filed "durable fix, owner-gated," check first whether the fix already EXISTS and is just unwired on the active code path — `grep` the function's callers before assuming new work; (4) glTF emissive_strength >1 that made the LEDs glow in F125 was also the bloom's HDR multiplier — the same knob fixes and breaks, so tune it against the reference luminance ratio, not "more glow." | 3D definitive crate bloomfix (F125-addendum, 2026-07-15) | unmined |
